The asymptotic behaviour of p p -capacitary potentials in Asymptotically Conical manifolds
Luca Benatti, Mattia Fogagnolo, Lorenzo Mazzieri

Abstract
. We study the asymptotic behaviour of the p p -capacitary potential and of the weak Inverse Mean Curvature Flow of a bounded set along the ends of an Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifolds with asymptotically nonnegative Ricci curvature.



MSC (2020): 58K55, 53E10, 31C12.

Keywords
: nonlinear potential theory, inverse mean curvature flow, asymptotic behaviour, partial differential equations.

1.
Introduction and statement of the main results

A natural question in the qualitative study of solutions to partial differential equations regards their behaviour at large distances on complete Riemannian manifolds. For harmonic potentials, a very satisfactory description was achieved in the fairly general framework of complete Riemannian manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature and Euclidean Volume Growth in
[
LTW97
]
and
[
CM97
]
. In
[
LTW97
]
using the representation formula and in
[
CM97
]
employing the monotonicity of the Almgren’s frequency function, the authors proved that the harmonic potential
u
of an open bounded subset
\Omega
with smooth boundary, namely the solution to

(1.1)

\left\{\kern-3.0pt\begin{array}[]{rcl@{\ \ }l}\Delta_{g}u&=&0&\text{ on $M\smallsetminus\overline{\Omega}$,}\\
u&=&1&\text{ on $\partial\Omega$,}\\
u(x)&\to&0&\text{ as $d_{g}(x,\Omega)\to+\infty$,}\end{array}\right.

is asymptotically equivalent to
d_{g}(x,\Omega)^{2-n}
, far away from
\Omega
(see also
[
Din02
,
AFM20
]
). In
[
AFM20
]
these results were applied to establish the Willmore Inequality in this framework and, consequently, a sharp Isoperimetric Inequality in dimension
n=3
with an explicit optimal constant depending only on the Asymptotic Volume Ratio (
\AVR(g)
for short) and the dimension of the manifold. The asymptotic behaviour of harmonic functions played a role also in the proof of the Positive Mass Theorem in
[
AMO21
]
in the context of Asymptotically Flat Riemannian manifolds with nonnegative scalar curvature.

In the last few years, it became evident that even stronger geometric conclusions can be drawn from the study of
p
-harmonic potentials on complete Riemannian manifolds, such as the validity of Minkowski Inequalities
[
FMP19
,
AFM22
,
BFM21
]
and the Riemannian Penrose Inequality
[
Ago+22
]
. Aim of the present work is to provide a detailed analysis of the asymptotic behaviour of these functions in the context of Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifolds. This study extends some classical results, obtained by Kichenassamy and Véron
[
KV86
]
(see also
[
Col+15
]
) in the context of the flat Euclidean space.

To state our results, we now introduce some notation and setup. We recall that on a Riemannian manifold
(M,g)
the
p
-capacitary potential of a bounded open domain
\Omega\subset M
is the solution
u:M\smallsetminus\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
to

(1.2)

\left\{\kern-3.0pt\begin{array}[]{rcl@{\ \ }l}\Delta^{\!(p)}_{g}u&=&0&\text{ on $M\smallsetminus\overline{\Omega}$,}\\
u&=&1&\text{ on $\partial\Omega$,}\\
u(x)&\to&0&\text{ as $d_{g}(x,\Omega)\to+\infty$,}\end{array}\right.

where
\Delta^{\!(p)}_{g}u=\div_{g}(\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}u$}}\right|^{p-2}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}u$}}\right|^{p-2}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}u$}}\right|^{p-2}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}u$}}\right|^{p-2}$}}{\rm D}u)
is the
p
-Laplace operator associated with the metric
g
.
Throughout the paper we will systematically work on complete noncompact Riemannian manifolds
(M,g)
of dimension
n\geq 3
that are
Asymptotically Conical
with
quadratically asymptotically nonnegative Ricci curvature
, that is

(1.3)

\displaystyle\Ric(x)\geq-\frac{(n-1)\kappa^{2}}{(1+d_{g}(x,o))^{2}}

for some some fixed
o\in M
and
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
, and every
x\in M
.
In accordance with
[
CEV17
]
, by the locution
Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifolds
, we are denoting, manifolds such that, outside of some open bounded subset
K
, are diffeomorphic to a (truncated) cone
[1,+\infty)\times L
, over a smooth hypersuface
L
, called the
link
of the cone, and such that the metric is close (in the
\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}
-topology for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\alpha\in[0,1]
) to the cone metric
\hat{g}={\,\rm d}\rho^{2}+\rho^{2}g_{L}
, where
\rho
is the radial coordinate on the cone (see
definition

2.13
for details).

Differently from the case
\Ric\geq 0
, where the Cheeger-Gromoll splitting theorem applies, here we possibly have to deal with manifolds that have more than one single end (see
[
LT92
, Definition 0.4 and discussion thereafter]
for the notion of ends). Nevertheless, due to the compactness and smoothness of
\partial K
, the manifold is forced to have a finite number of
ends

E_{1},\ldots,E_{N}
.
We can assume that each end
E_{i}
is diffeomorphic to
[1,+\infty)\times L_{i}
for every
i=1,\ldots,N
, being
L_{i}
the connected components of
L
, and consequently each end is Asymptotically Conical. We can define an Asymptotic Volume Ratio on each end as

(1.4)

\AVR(g;E_{i})=\lim_{R\to+\infty}\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle B(o,R)\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle B(o,R)\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle B(o,R)\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.06995pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.06995pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B(o,R)\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}R^{n}}.

Indeed, it is not hard to realise that, even if it is not monotone, the ratio
\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle B(o,R)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle B(o,R)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle B(o,R)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B(o,R)$}}\right|$}}/R^{n}
has a limit as
R\to+\infty
in Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifolds. Moreover, the Asymptotic Volume Ratio
\AVR(g)
of the manifold splits as

(1.5)

\AVR(g)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\AVR(g;E_{i})

where
0<\AVR(g)\leq N
is the Asymptotic Volume Ratio of
(M,g)
.

It is not surprising that the asymptotic behaviour of the solution to
eq.

1.2
could be different depending on the end, and in fact it turns out that the behaviour on a given end it is not affected by what happens on the others. More precisely, observing that for large enough
T
the set
\mathchoice{\left\{{u>1/T}\right\}}{\{{u>1/T}\}}{\left\{{u>1/T}\right\}}{\left\{{u>1/T}\right\}}
contains
K
, we can define the
normalised
p
-capacity of
\Omega
relative to the end
E_{i}
as

(1.6)

\mathrm{C}^{(i)}_{p}(\Omega)=\left(\frac{p-1}{n-p}\right)^{p-1}\frac{1}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}\int\limits_{\mathchoice{\left\{{u=1/t}\right\}}{\{{u=1/t}\}}{\left\{{u=1/t}\right\}}{\left\{{u=1/t}\right\}}\cap E_{i}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}u$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}u$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}u$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}u$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\,\rm d}\sigma\,,

for every
i=1,\ldots,m
. Observe that, by virtue of the divergence theorem, the right hand side does not depend on
t>T
and the above formula yields a well posed definition. The
normalised
p
-capacity of
\Omega
(see
definition

2.10
) then split as

(1.7)

\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathrm{C}^{(i)}_{p}(\Omega).

We are now ready to state our first main result.

Theorem 1.1

(Asymptotic behaviour of the
p
-capacitary potential)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifold with Ricci curvature satisfying
eq.

1.3
. Let
E_{1},\ldots,E_{N}
be the (finitely many) ends of
M
with respect to the compact
K
in
definition

2.13
. Consider
\Omega\subset M
be an open bounded subset with smooth boundary and
u:M\smallsetminus\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
the solution to the problem (
1.2
). Then

(1.8)

\displaystyle u(x)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}^{(i)}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g;E_{i})}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\rho(x)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}+o\left(\rho(x)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}\right)

on
E_{i}
as
\rho(x)\to+\infty
for every
i=1,\ldots,N
.

When the
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical condition is strengthen to
\mathscr{C}^{k}
-Asymptotically Conical condition, it is not difficult to deduce corresponding asymptotic behaviours for the derivatives up to order
k
of
u
(see
theorem

3.1
below). Our result extends to the nonlinear setting the asymptotic analyses carried out in
[
AMO22
, Theorem 2.2]
,
[
AMO21
, Lemma 2.2]
,
[
HM20
, Lemma 4.1]
and
[
MMT20
, Lemma A.2]
, although without refined estimates of the error terms. It would be interesting to deal, building on
theorem

1.1
, with such remainders, possibly following the lines of
[
Chr90
]
.

We emphasise that
theorem

1.1
plays a crucial role in the recent proof of the Riemannian Penrose Inequality through
p
-harmonic potentials proposed in
[
Ago+22
]
and that it will be employed to provide new results in this field under milder asymptotic conditions. To this end, we point out that the requirements above do not involve explicit rates of decay to the reference metric, that are usually assumed when dealing with these topics.

It turns out that our approach, employed for proving
theorem

1.1
and its consequences, appearing in
section

3
, happen to fit also the geometric case of the weak Inverse Mean Curvature Flow starting at a bounded
\Omega\subset M
with smooth boundary.
We briefly recall that with this notion, introduced by Huisken and Ilmanen
[
HI01
]
as a weak counterpart to the classical evolution by inverse mean curvature
[
Ger90
,
Urb90
]
, it is indicated a Lipschitz function
w\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\mathrm{loc}}

that satisfies

(1.9)

\mathrm{div}\left(\frac{\mathrm{D}w}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}w$}}\right|$}}}\right)=\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}w$}}\right|$}}

on
M\setminus\overline{\Omega}
and such that
\Omega=\{w<0\}
in a very geometric nonstandard weak variational sense. By the pioneering work of Moser
[
Mos07
,
Mos08
]
and subsequent extensions to Riemannian manifolds
[
KN09
,
MRS21
]
, the solution
w
can also be interpreted as the locally uniform limit as
p\to 1^{+}
of
-(p-1)\log u_{p}
, where
u_{p}
is the
p
-capacitary potential of
\Omega
.

In analogy with
eq.

1.6
, we set

(1.10)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}=\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\mathchoice{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\{{w\leq t}\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\mathchoice{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\{{w\leq t}\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\mathchoice{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\{{w\leq t}\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\mathchoice{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\{{w\leq t}\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}}{\mathrm{e}^{t}},

for every
i=1,\ldots,N
and every
t\geq T
, where
T
is so chosen that
\mathchoice{\left\{{w\geq T}\right\}}{\{{w\geq T}\}}{\left\{{w\geq T}\right\}}{\left\{{w\geq T}\right\}}
contains
K
.
By means of
[
HI01
, Exponential Growth Lemma 2.6]
and the divergence theorem, we have that the right hand side does not depend on
t>T
and yields a well posed definition. As for the
p
-capacity, we have that

(1.11)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}

where
\Omega^{*}
is the strictly outward minimising hull of
\Omega

[
FM20
]
. We refer to
section

4
for a more detailed discussion.
The following theorem provides a description of the asymptotic behaviour for the weak IMCF for large times.

Theorem 1.2

(Asymptotic behaviour of the Inverse Mean Curvature Flow)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete
\mathscr{C}^{1}
-Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifold with Ricci curvature satisfying
eq.

1.3
. Let
E_{1},\ldots,E_{N}
be the (finitely many) ends of
M
with respect to the compact
K
in
definition

2.13
. Consider
\Omega\subset M
be an open bounded subset with smooth boundary and
w:M\smallsetminus\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
be the weak Inverse Mean Curvature Flow starting at
\Omega
. Then

(1.12)

\displaystyle w(x)

\displaystyle=\log\left(\rho(x)^{n-1}\right)-\log\left(\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}}{\AVR(g;E_{i})\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}\right)+o\left(1\right)

on
E_{i}
as
\rho(x)\to+\infty
for every
i=1,\ldots,N
.

It might be useful to observe that, coherently, multiplying by
-(p-1)
the logarithm of the right hand side of
eq.

1.8
and letting
p\to 1^{+}
one exactly recovers the right hand side of
eq.

1.12
. In fact, as a consequence of
[
FM20
, Theorem 1.2]
, we have that

(1.13)

\lim_{p\to 1^{+}}\mathrm{C}^{(i)}_{p}(\Omega)=\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}

holds for every
i=1,\ldots,N
(see
lemma

4.4
below). Observe that, differently from
theorem

1.1
we required
\mathscr{C}^{1}
-convergence of the metric. This additional requirement is due to the fact that, up to the authors’ knowledge, a Cheng-Yau-type estimate with sharp decay for the gradient of the IMCF is not known. Therefore, in our proof we use the gradient bound
[
HI01
, Weak Existence Theorem 3.1]
. In some cases, for example
\Ric\geq 0
, this requirement can be weakened in favour of the
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-convergence (see the discussion before
proposition

4.2
). To our knowledge,
theorem

1.2
with the explicit constant in the expansion (
1.12
) was known only in the flat case of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and was obtained by completely different means. Indeed, in this setting, the level sets of the weak IMCF become starshaped (and thus smooth) after a sufficiently long time, as a consequence of
[
HI08
, Theorem 2.7]
. At this point,
eq.

1.12
could be easily deduced by classical results
[
Ger90
,
Urb90
]
for the smooth IMCF. It is worth pointing out that the arguments we employ got an important inspiration also from those in the proof of
[
HI01
, Blowdown Lemma 7.1]
, that actually helped also in establishing
theorem

1.1
.
theorem

1.2
provided a simplified approach to the proof of
[
HI01
, Blowdown Lemma 7.1]
. At the same time extend such result to the class of Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifolds, adding the explicit characterisation of the constant term in the expansion (
1.12
).

The results above in particular apply to Asymptotically Locally Euclidean spaces (ALE for short)
gravitational instantons
, that are noncompact hyperkhäler Ricci Flat
4
-dimensional manifolds playing a role in the study of Euclidean Quantum Gravity Theory, Gauge Theory and String Theory (see
[
Haw77
,
EH79
,
Kro89
,
Kro89a
,
Min09
,
Min10
,
Min11
]
). Moreover, it is not difficult to realise that the completeness assumption can be dropped, and that the above results can be extended to manifolds with boundary. Indeed, the method proposed is completely blind to everything is inside
\Omega
, and, therefore, we can also include Asymptotically Flat Riemannian manifolds
(M,g)
with compact boundary
\partial M
, of fundamental importance in General Relativity.

Summary.
The paper is organised as follows. In
section

2
, we recall some basic notions about
p
-harmonic functions and the
p
-capacitary potential as well as an improvement of Li-Yau-type estimates holding true on Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifolds satisfying the bound
eq.

1.3
, with controlled constants as
p\to 1^{+}
.
section

3
and
section

4
are devoted to the proof of the asymptotic behaviour of the
p
-capacitary potential and of the (weak) IMCF, that are respectively
theorem

1.1
and
theorem

1.2
, together with some other related results. In the last section we prove the sharpness of the Minkowski Inequality in Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature and its rigidity statement in the general Euclidean volume growth case.

Acknowledgements.

The authors are grateful to V. Agostiniani, F. Oronzio, G. Antonelli for precious discussions and comments during the preparation of this manuscript. The authors are members of Gruppo Nazionale per l’Analisi Matematica, la Probabilità e le loro Applicazioni (GNAMPA).

2.
Preliminary results on
p
-capacitary potentials

2.1.
p
-harmonic functions and regularity

Before considering the specific case of problem (
1.2
), we recall the definition of
p
-harmonic functions, as well as their regularity estimates. Given an open subset
U
of a complete Riemannian manifold
(M,g)
, we say that
v\in W^{1,p}(U)
is
p
-harmonic if

(2.1)

\int\limits_{U}\left\langle\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p-2}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p-2}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p-2}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p-2}$}}{\rm D}v\,|\,{\rm D}\psi\right\rangle{\,\rm d}\mu=0.

for any test function
\psi\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}(U)
. With
\langle\,\cdot\,|\,\cdot\,\rangle
we denote as usual the scalar product induced by the underlying Riemannian metric
g
on the tangent space at each point.

We now precisely recall what is known about the regularity of
p
-harmonic functions.
Suppose by now that
K\Subset U
is entirely contained in a chart of
M
. For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
, we have that

(2.2)

\displaystyle g_{ij}(x)\xi^{i}\xi^{j}>\lambda\delta_{ij}\xi^{i}\xi^{j}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\|{{g_{ij}}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}(K)$}}}}{\|{{g_{ij}}}\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}(K)$}}}}{\left\|{{g_{ij}}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}(K)$}}}}{\left\|{{g_{ij}}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}(K)$}}}}<\Lambda_{k,\alpha}

holds for every
x\in K
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
for some positive constants
\lambda
and
\Lambda_{k,\alpha}
. Regularity results for
p
-harmonic functions (see
[
Tol83
,
DiB83
,
Lie88
]
) yield

(2.3)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v(x)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v(x)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v(x)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v(x)$}}\right|$}}\leq\mathrm{C}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v(x)-{\rm D}v(y)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v(x)-{\rm D}v(y)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v(x)-{\rm D}v(y)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v(x)-{\rm D}v(y)$}}\right|$}}\leq\mathrm{C}\,\left(\vphantom{\sum}d_{g}(x,y)\right)^{\beta}

for every
p
-harmonic function
v
with
\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle v$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle v$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle v$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle v$}}\right|$}}\leq 1
, with
\mathrm{C}
,
\beta
depending only on
n
,
p
,
\alpha
,
\lambda
and
\Lambda_{0,\alpha}
and the distance of
K
from the boundary of
U
. For a general
K
, since a compact set can be finitely covered by charts, the result can be extended defining, with abuse of notation,
\lambda
and
\Lambda_{k,\alpha}
as the minimal
\lambda
and maximal
\Lambda_{k,\alpha}
in (
2.2
) among a family of charts covering
K
. Hence, the theorem below easily follows by a scaling argument, being that
v/\mathchoice{\left\|{{v}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}}}{\|{{v}}\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}}}{\left\|{{v}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}}}{\left\|{{v}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}}}
is
p
-harmonic as well.

Theorem 2.1

(Schauder interior estimates)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete Riemannian manifold of dimension
n
,
U\subseteq M
be an open subset and let
1<p
. For any
\alpha\in(0,1)
and
K\Subset U
, there exists a positive constant
\beta=\beta(n,p,\alpha,\Lambda_{0,\alpha},\lambda)\in(0,\alpha)
such that any bounded solution
v:U\to\mathbb{R}
of the problem
\Delta_{g}^{\!(p)}v=0
on
U
belongs to
\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}(K)
. Moreover, there is a positive constant
\mathrm{C}=\mathrm{C}(n,p,d_{g}(K,\partial U),\Lambda_{0,\alpha},\lambda)
such that

(2.4)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v(x)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v(x)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v(x)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v(x)$}}\right|$}}\leq\mathrm{C}\mathchoice{\left\|{{v}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}}}{\|{{v}}\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}}}{\left\|{{v}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}}}{\left\|{{v}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}}}

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v(x)-{\rm D}v(y)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v(x)-{\rm D}v(y)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v(x)-{\rm D}v(y)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v(x)-{\rm D}v(y)$}}\right|$}}\leq\mathrm{C}\mathchoice{\left\|{{v}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}}}{\|{{v}}\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}}}{\left\|{{v}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}}}{\left\|{{v}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}}}\,\left(\vphantom{\sum}d_{g}(x,y)\right)^{\beta}

for any
x,y\in K
.

On the other hand, the classical regularity theory for quasilinear nondegenerate elliptic equations ensures that Sobolev functions satisfying (
2.1
) are smooth around the points where the gradient does not vanish (see
[
LSU68
, Chapter 4, Section 6]
). Moreover, the classical elliptic regularity theory can be applied to get higher order interior estimates (see
[
GT15
, Theorem 6.6]
).

Theorem 2.2

(Higher order Schauder estimates)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a Riemannian manifold of dimension
n
,
U\subseteq M
be an open smooth subset of
M
and consider
1<p<n
. Then for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\beta\in(0,1)
and
K\Subset U
, and every bounded solution
v:U\to\mathbb{R}
of the problem
\Delta_{g}^{\!(p)}v=0
on
U
such that
\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|$}}\geq m>0
on
K
, there exists a constant
\mathrm{C}_{k,\beta}=\mathrm{C}_{k,\beta}(n,p,d_{g}(K,\partial U),\Lambda_{k-1,\beta},\lambda,m)
such that

(2.5)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\|{{v}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\beta}(K)$}}}}{\|{{v}}\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\beta}(K)$}}}}{\left\|{{v}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\beta}(K)$}}}}{\left\|{{v}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k,\beta}(K)$}}}}\leq\mathrm{C}_{k,\beta}\mathchoice{\left\|{{v}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}}}{\|{{v}}\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}}}{\left\|{{v}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}}}{\left\|{{v}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle L^{\infty}(U)$}}}}.

Given
U\subseteq M
with Lipschitz boundary, a
p
-harmonic function
u\in W^{1,p}(U)
attains some Dirichlet data
g\in L^{p}(\partial U)
if
u
coincides with
g
on
\partial U
in the sense of the trace operator. We report in the next remark the issue of the boundary regularity.

Remark 2.3

(Boundary regularity of
p
-harmonic functions)
.

We point out that, if a
p
-harmonic function attains some
\mathscr{C}^{1,\alpha}
-Dirichlet data on a
\mathscr{C}^{1,\alpha}
boundary, then the
\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}
-estimates of
theorem

2.1
can be extended up to the boundary. This is a major contribution of
[
Lie88
]
. Moreover, if its gradient does not vanish at the boundary the function is smooth up to the boundary and
theorem

2.2
extends as well.

We finally retrieve the Comparison Principles
[
Tol83
, Lemma 3.1, Proposition 3.3.2]
by Tolksdorf, specialised for our purposes.

Theorem 2.4

(Comparison Principles)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete Riemannian manifold,
U\subseteq M
be an open bounded subset and
v_{1},v_{2}:U\to\mathbb{R}
be two
p
-harmonic functions.

•

(Weak) Comparison Principle.
If
v_{1},v_{2}\in\mathscr{C}^{0}(\overline{U})
and
v_{1}\leq v_{2}
on
\partial U
, then
v_{1}\leq v_{2}
on
U
.

•

Strong Comparison Principle.
Suppose in addition that
U
is connected,
v_{1}\in\mathscr{C}^{1}(\overline{U})
,
v_{2}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{U})
and
\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\nabla v_{2}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\nabla v_{2}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\nabla v_{2}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.12502pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.12502pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\nabla v_{2}$}}\right|$}}\geq\delta>0
in
U
. If
v_{1}\leq v_{2}
(resp.
v_{1}\geq v_{2}
) on
U
, then
v_{1}=v_{2}
or
v_{1}<v_{2}
(resp.
v_{1}>v_{2}
) on
U
.

To conclude, we want to recall a compactness theorem that holds for
p
-harmonic functions. It is a natural question whether the limit of a sequence of
p
-harmonic functions is still
p
-harmonic. Clearly the weak formulation in (
2.1
) suggests that
\mathscr{C}^{1}
-convergence on compact subsets is enough to ensure that also the limit function is
p
-harmonic. The following theorem relaxes this hypothesis in favour of uniform convergence on compact subsets.

Theorem 2.5

(Compactness Theorem)
.

Let
(v_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of
p
-harmonic functions on
U
that converges uniformly to
v
on compact subsets of
U
as
n\to+\infty
. Then
v\in W^{1,p}_{\mathrm{loc}}
is
p
-harmonic on
U
.

See
[
HK88
, Theorem 3.2]
.∎

Remark 2.6
.

Suppose that
(U_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of open subsets converging to
U
open subset as
n\to+\infty
. Let
g_{n}
be a metric on
U_{n}
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
that locally uniformly converges to some metric
g
on
U
as
n\to+\infty
. The above theorem still holds if
v_{n}
is
p
-harmonic with respect to the metric
g_{n}
. Consequently,
v
is
p
-harmonic on
U
with respect to
g
.

2.2.
p
-nonparabolic manifolds and the
p
-capacitary potential

We analyse here the existence and uniqueness of solution
u_{p}
to (
1.2
) on complete Riemannian manifolds. Given a noncompact Riemannian manifold
M
, we consider the
p
-capacitary potential of a bounded set with smooth boundary
\Omega\subset M
, that is a function
u\in W^{1,p}_{\mathrm{loc}}(M\smallsetminus\Omega)
solving (
1.2
). The regularity results previously discussed ensure that
u
belongs to
\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(M\smallsetminus\Omega)
and it is smooth near the points where the gradient does not vanish. In particular, by Hopf Maximum Principle
[
Tol83
]
the datum on
\partial\Omega
is attained smoothly.

We now focus on some classical sufficient conditions to ensure the existence of the
p
-capacitary potential, which turns out to be related to the notion of
p
-Green’s function we are going to recall.

Definition 2.7

(
p
-Green’s function)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete Riemannian manifold. Let
\mathrm{Diag}(M)=\{(x,x)\in M\times M\,|\,x\in M\}
. For
p\geq 1
, we say that
G_{p}:M\times M\smallsetminus\mathrm{Diag}(M)\to\mathbb{R}
is a
p
-Green’s function
for
M
if
it weakly satisfies
\Delta_{p}G(o,\,\cdot\,)=-\delta_{o}
for any
o\in M
, where
\delta_{o}
is the Dirac delta centred at
o
, that is, if it holds

(2.6)

\int\limits_{M}\Big\langle\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.31946pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.31946pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\,G_{p}{(o,\,\cdot\,)}$}}\right|^{p-2}$}}{\raisebox{2.31946pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.31946pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\,G_{p}{(o,\,\cdot\,)}$}}\right|^{p-2}$}}{\raisebox{1.6389pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.6389pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\,G_{p}{(o,\,\cdot\,)}$}}\right|^{p-2}$}}{\raisebox{1.0139pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.0139pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\,G_{p}{(o,\,\cdot\,)}$}}\right|^{p-2}$}}\,{\rm D}\,G_{p}{(o,\,\cdot\,)}\,\Big|\,{\rm D}\psi\Big\rangle{\,\rm d}\mu=\psi(o)

for any
\psi\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{c}(M)
.

The notion of
p
-Green’s function immediately calls for that of
p
-nonparabolic Riemannian manifold.

Definition 2.8

(
p
-nonparabolicity)
.

We say that a complete noncompact Riemannian manifold
(M,g)
is
p
-nonparabolic if, for any
o\in M
, there exists a
positive

p
-Green’s function
G_{p}:M\smallsetminus\{o\}\to\mathbb{R}
. With the expression
p
-Green function
we are in fact referring to the positive minimal one.

The notion of
p
-nonparabolicity is intimately related to existence of a solution to (
1.2
), in that if the
p
-Green’s function of a
p
-nonparabolic Riemannian manifold vanishes at infinity, then such solution exists for any open bounded subset
\Omega\subset M
with smooth boundary. A complete and self contained proof of this fact is provided in the Appendix of
[
FM20
]
. We report the statement of such basic though fundamental result.

Theorem 2.9

(Existence of the
p
-capacitary potential)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete noncompact
p
-nonparabolic Riemannian manifold. Assume also that the
p
-Green’s function
G_{p}
satisfies
G_{p}(o,x)\to 0
as
d_{g}(o,x)\to+\infty
for some
o\in M
. Let
\Omega\subset M
be an open bounded subset with smooth boundary. Then, there exists a unique solution
u_{p}
to (
1.2
).

We want to underline that, the existence of a
p
-Green’s function does not guarantee that it vanishes at infinity. This last property is related to the geometry of all ends. We refer the reader to
[
Hol90
,
Hol99
]
for a detailed discussion on this topic.

It is convenient to recall here the definition of
p
-capacity of an open bounded subset
\Omega\subset M
together with a normalised version of it which turns out to be more convenient for our computations.

Definition 2.10

(
p
-capacity and normalised
p
-capacity)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete noncompact Riemannian manifold, and let
\Omega
be an open bounded subset of
M
.
The
p
-capacity
of
\Omega
is defined as

(2.7)

\mathrm{Cap}_{p}(\Omega)=\inf\mathchoice{\left\{{\,\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}\mu\left.\vphantom{\,\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}\mu v\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}(M),\,v\geq 1\text{ on }\Omega}\right|v\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}(M),\,v\geq 1\text{ on }\Omega}\right\}}{\{{\,\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}\mu\left.\vphantom{\,\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}\mu v\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}(M),\,v\geq 1\text{ on }\Omega}\right|v\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}(M),\,v\geq 1\text{ on }\Omega}\}}{\left\{{\,\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}\mu\left.\vphantom{\,\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm }\Omega}\right|v\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}(M),\,v\geq 1\text{ on }\Omega}\right\}}{\left\{{\,\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}\mu\left.\vphantom{\,\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}\mu v\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}(M),\,v\geq 1\text{ on }\Omega}\right|v\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}(M),\,v\geq 1\text{ on }\Omega}\right\}}.

On the other hand, the
normalised
p
-capacity
of
\Omega
is defined as

(2.8)

A function
u
solving (
1.2
) realises the
p
-capacity of the initial set
\Omega
, and actually one can also characterise such quantity with a suitable integral on
\partial\Omega
. We resume these facts in the following statements, whose proof can be found in
[
BFM21
, Section 2.2]

Proposition 2.11
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete noncompact
p
-nonparabolic Riemannian manifold. Assume also that the
p
-Green’s function
G_{p}
satisfies
G_{p}(o,x)\to 0
as
d_{g}(o,x)\to+\infty
for some
o\in M
. Let
\Omega\subset M
be an open bounded subset with smooth boundary. Then the solution
u_{p}
to (
1.2
) realises

(2.9)

\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)=\left(\frac{p-1}{n-p}\right)^{p-1}\frac{1}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}\int\limits_{M\smallsetminus\overline{\Omega}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.31946pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.31946pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{2.31946pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.31946pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.6389pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.6389pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.0139pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.0139pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}\mu

Moreover, we have that

(2.10)

\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)=\left(\frac{p-1}{n-p}\right)^{p-1}\frac{1}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}\int\limits_{\mathchoice{\left\{{u_{p}=1/t}\right\}}{\{{u_{p}=1/t}\}}{\left\{{u_{p}=1/t}\right\}}{\left\{{u_{p}=1/t}\right\}}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.31946pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.31946pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{2.31946pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.31946pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{1.6389pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.6389pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{1.0139pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.0139pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\,\rm d}\sigma.

holds for almost every
t\in[1,+\infty)
, including any
1/t
regular value for
u_{p}
.

In particular, evaluating (
2.10
) at
t=1
, which is a regular value by the Hopf Maximum Principle, we have that

(2.11)

\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)=\left(\frac{p-1}{n-p}\right)^{p-1}\frac{1}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}\int\limits_{\partial\Omega}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.31946pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.31946pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{2.31946pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.31946pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{1.6389pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.6389pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{1.0139pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.0139pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\,\rm d}\sigma.

Moreover, one can actually relate the capacity of
\Omega_{t}=\mathchoice{\left\{{u>1/t}\right\}}{\{{u>1/t}\}}{\left\{{u>1/t}\right\}}{\left\{{u>1/t}\right\}}\cup\Omega
to the capacity of
\Omega
.

Proposition 2.12
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete noncompact
p
-nonparabolic Riemannian manifold, for some
p>1
. Let
\Omega\subset M
be an open bounded subset with smooth boundary. Then the solution
u_{p}
to (
1.2
) realises

(2.12)

\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega_{t})=t^{p-1}\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)

for every
t\in[1,+\infty)
, where
\Omega_{t}=\mathchoice{\left\{{u>1/t}\right\}}{\{{u>1/t}\}}{\left\{{u>1/t}\right\}}{\left\{{u>1/t}\right\}}\cup\Omega
. In particular, the map
t\mapsto\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega_{t})
is smooth.

From now on, unless where it is necessary, we fix
1<p
and we drop the subscript
p
when we consider a solution
u_{p}
to the problem (
1.2
).

Given an end
E\subset M
and a set
\Omega\subset M
such that
\partial E\subset\Omega
we define the capacity of
\Omega
restricted to
E
as

(2.13)

{\rm Cap}_{p}(\Omega;E)=\inf\mathchoice{\left\{{\,\int\limits_{E}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm d}\mu\left.\vphantom{\,\int\limits_{E}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}\mu v\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}(M),\,v\geq 1\text{ on }\Omega}\right|v\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}(M),\,v\geq 1\text{ on }\Omega}\right\}}{\{{\,\int\limits_{E}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm d}\mu\left.\vphantom{\,\int\limits_{E}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}\mu v\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}(M),\,v\geq 1\text{ on }\Omega}\right|v\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{\infty}(M),\,v\geq 1\text{ on }\Omega}\}}{\left\{{\,\int\limits_{E}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm The set function
{\rm Cap}_{p}(\,\cdot\,;E)
has the same properties of the standard
p
-capacity in
definition

2.10
.

2.3.
Preliminary properties of Asymptotically Conical manifolds

We give here the precise definition of Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifolds according to
[
CEV17
]
. For better comprehension, we recall the definition of the Hölder seminorm of a tensor field. A tensor field
T\in\mathcal{T}_{s}^{q}(M)
is
\alpha
-Hölder continuous at
x
for some
\alpha\in[0,1]
if there exists a geodesically convex open neighbourhood
U_{x}
centred at
x
such that

(2.14)

\displaystyle\sup_{y\in U_{x}\smallsetminus\mathchoice{\left\{{x}\right\}}{\{{x}\}}{\left\{{x}\right\}}{\left\{{x}\right\}}}\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle T(x)-T(y)$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle T(x)-T(y)$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle T(x)-T(y)$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle T(x)-T(y)$}}\right|_{g}$}}}{\left(d_{g}(x,y)\right)^{\alpha}}<+\infty

is finite, where, to compute the difference between
T(x)
and
T(y)
, we parallel transport
T(y)
onto
x
. The tensor field
T
is said to be
\alpha
-Hölder continuous on
U\subset M
if it is
\alpha
-Hölder continuous at every
x\in U
. We sometimes omit the subscript
g
if it is clear the metric we are referring to.

Consider a
cone with link
L
, namely
((0,+\infty)\times L,\hat{g})
where
\hat{g}={\,\rm d}\rho^{2}+\rho^{2}g_{L}
. In this case, let
s>0
be such that
B_{s}(x)
is geodesically convex in
((0,+\infty)\times L,\hat{g})
for every
x\in\mathchoice{\left\{{1}\right\}}{\{{1}\}}{\left\{{1}\right\}}{\left\{{1}\right\}}\times L
. Then, for every
x\in(0,+\infty)\times L
the ball of radius
s\rho(x)
centred at
x
is still geodesically convex, where
\rho:(0,+\infty)\times L\to(0,+\infty)
is the projection onto the first coordinate. Given an
\alpha
-Hölder continuous tensor field
T
, we define the
\alpha
-Hölder seminorm of
T
at
x
as

(2.15)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left[{{T}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}^{(s)}}{[{{T}}]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}^{(s)}}{\left[{{T}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}^{(s)}}{\left[{{T}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}^{(s)}}(x)=\sup_{y\in B_{s\rho(x)}(x)\smallsetminus\mathchoice{\left\{{x}\right\}}{\{{x}\}}{\left\{{x}\right\}}{\left\{{x}\right\}}}\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle T(x)-T(y)$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle T(x)-T(y)$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle T(x)-T(y)$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle T(x)-T(y)$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}}{\left(\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(x,y)\right)^{\alpha}}.

Observe that, if
T
is bounded (with respect to
\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\,\cdot\,$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}
) and
s,t>0
satisfy the above assumptions,
\mathchoice{\left[{{T}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}^{(s)}}{[{{T}}]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}^{(s)}}{\left[{{T}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}^{(s)}}{\left[{{T}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}^{(s)}}(x)=\mathchoice{\left[{{T}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}^{(t)}}{[{{T}}]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}^{(t)}}{\left[{{T}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}^{(t)}}{\left[{{T}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}^{(t)}}(x)
for any
x\in(R,+\infty)\times L
provided
R
is large enough. Then, the following definition is well-posed and we can drop the superscript
(s)
.

Definition 2.13

(
\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}
-Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifolds)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a Riemannian manifold,
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\alpha\in[0,1)
.
M
is said to be
\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}
-Asymptotically Conical
if there exists an open bounded subset
K\subseteq M
, a closed smooth hypersurface
L
and a diffeomorphism
\pi:M\smallsetminus K\to[1,+\infty)\times L
such that

(2.16)

\sum_{i=0}^{k}\rho^{i}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.80057pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.80057pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}^{(i)}_{\hat{g}}(\pi_{*}g-\hat{g})$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{2.55057pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.55057pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}^{(i)}_{\hat{g}}(\pi_{*}g-\hat{g})$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{1.20557pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.20557pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}^{(i)}_{\hat{g}}(\pi_{*}g-\hat{g})$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{0.86111pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-0.86111pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}^{(i)}_{\hat{g}}(\pi_{*}g-\hat{g})$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}+\rho^{k+\alpha}\mathchoice{\left[{{{\rm D}^{(k)}_{\hat{g}}(\pi_{*}g-\hat{g})}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}{[{{{\rm D}^{(k)}_{\hat{g}}(\pi_{*}g-\hat{g})}}]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}{\left[{{{\rm D}^{(k)}_{\hat{g}}(\pi_{*}g-\hat{g})}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}{\left[{{{\rm D}^{(k)}_{\hat{g}}(\pi_{*}g-\hat{g})}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}=o(1),

as
\rho\to+\infty
, where
\rho:[1,+\infty)\times L\to[1,+\infty)
is the projection map onto the first component and
\hat{g}={\,\rm d}\rho^{2}+\rho^{2}g_{L}
is the cone metric. We use the convention
\mathscr{C}^{k}=\mathscr{C}^{k,0}
.

definition

2.13
says that in a
\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}
-Asymptotically Conical Manifold the metric
g
approaches the metric
\hat{g}
of a
truncated cone with link
L
with respect to a scaling invariant
\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}
-norm. The diffeomorphism
\pi:M\smallsetminus K\to[1,+\infty)\times L
identifies the boundary of
K
with the link
L
. With abuse of notation,
\pi_{*}\rho:M\smallsetminus K\to[1,+\infty)
will be denoted by
\rho
and
\pi_{*}\hat{g}={\,\rm d}\rho^{2}+\rho^{2}g_{L}
by
\hat{g}
. Moreover, by convention the set
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho<1}\right\}}{\{{\rho<1}\}}{\left\{{\rho<1}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho<1}\right\}}
is used to denote
K
and accordingly
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq r}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}=M\smallsetminus\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho>r}\right\}}{\{{\rho>r}\}}{\left\{{\rho>r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho>r}\right\}}
and
\mathchoice{\left\{{1\leq\rho\leq r}\right\}}{\{{1\leq\rho\leq r}\}}{\left\{{1\leq\rho\leq r}\right\}}{\left\{{1\leq\rho\leq r}\right\}}=M\smallsetminus(\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho>r}\right\}}{\{{\rho>r}\}}{\left\{{\rho>r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho>r}\right\}}\cup K)
. Given any coordinate system
(\vartheta^{1},\ldots,\vartheta^{n-1})
on an open subset
U
of
L
,
(\rho,\vartheta^{1},\ldots,\vartheta^{n-1})
are coordinates on
(1,+\infty)\times U\subset M\smallsetminus\overline{K}
. The condition
\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle g-\hat{g}$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle g-\hat{g}$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{2.73611pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.73611pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle g-\hat{g}$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{2.98611pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.98611pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle g-\hat{g}$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}=o(1)
as
\rho\to+\infty
is equivalent to a condition on the coordinates that can be read as

(2.17)

\displaystyle g_{\rho\rho}=1+o(1)

\displaystyle g_{\rho j}=o(\rho)

\displaystyle g_{ij}=\rho^{2}g^{L}_{ij}+o(\rho^{2})

for every
i,j=1,\ldots,n-1
as
\rho\to+\infty
. By using Cramer’s rule to solve the system and Laplace expansion to compute determinants, we obtain

(2.18)

\displaystyle g^{\rho\rho}=1+o(1)

\displaystyle g^{\rho j}=o(\rho^{-1})

\displaystyle g^{ij}={\rho^{-2}}g_{L}^{ij}+o(\rho^{-2}).

The
\mathscr{C}^{0,\alpha}
-Asymptotically Conical condition for
\alpha>0
gives, in addition, information on the Hölder seminorm of the components. Indeed, arguing as before we get that

(2.19)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left[{{g_{\rho\rho}-1}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}{[{{g_{\rho\rho}-1}}]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}{\left[{{g_{\rho\rho}-1}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}{\left[{{g_{\rho\rho}-1}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}=o(\rho^{-\alpha})

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left[{{g_{\rho j}}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}{[{{g_{\rho j}}}]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}{\left[{{g_{\rho j}}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}{\left[{{g_{\rho j}}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}=o(\rho^{1-\alpha})

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left[{{g_{ij}-\rho^{2}g^{L}_{ij}}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}{[{{g_{ij}-\rho^{2}g^{L}_{ij}}}]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}{\left[{{g_{ij}-\rho^{2}g^{L}_{ij}}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}{\left[{{g_{ij}-\rho^{2}g^{L}_{ij}}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}=o(\rho^{2-\alpha})

for every
i,j=1,\ldots,n-1
as
\rho\to+\infty
. Increasing
k
in the
\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}
-Asymptotically Conical assumption we gain knowledge about the
k
-th derivative of the components of
g
. Increasing
k
in the
\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}
-Asymptotically Conical assumption we gain knowledge about the
k
-th derivative of
(g-\hat{g})
.

Consider for every
s>0
the family of diffeomorphism on
(0,+\infty)\times L
defined as

(2.20)

\begin{array}[]{rlcl}\omega_{s}:&(0,+\infty)\times L&\longrightarrow&(0,+\infty)\times L\\[5.69046pt]
&(\rho,\vartheta^{1},\ldots,\vartheta^{n-1})&\longmapsto&(s\rho,\vartheta^{1},\ldots,\vartheta^{n-1}),\end{array}

With abuse of language we will also denote by
\omega_{s}
any restriction of it to some truncated cone. Since
\omega_{s}
induces a family of diffeomorphisms from
(1/s,+\infty)\times L
onto
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\geq 1}\right\}}{\{{\rho\geq 1}\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq 1}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq 1}\right\}}\subset M
through the composition with
\pi
in
definition

2.13
, we will also denote by
\omega_{s}
such map. The condition (
2.16
) can be also interpreted as the convergence of the family of metrics on the cone
(0,+\infty)\times L
, built for every
s\geq 1
by pulling the metric
g
back through the diffeomorphism
\omega_{s}
and properly rescaling them. This is the content of the following lemma.

Lemma 2.14
.

A complete Riemannian manifold
(M,g)
is
\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}
-Asymptotically Conical if and only if the metric
g_{(s)}=s^{-2}\omega_{s}^{*}g
satisfies

(2.21)

\sum_{i=0}^{k}\rho^{i}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.80057pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.80057pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}^{(i)}_{\hat{g}}(g_{(s)}-\hat{g})$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{2.55057pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.55057pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}^{(i)}_{\hat{g}}(g_{(s)}-\hat{g})$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{1.20557pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.20557pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}^{(i)}_{\hat{g}}(g_{(s)}-\hat{g})$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{0.86111pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-0.86111pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}^{(i)}_{\hat{g}}(g_{(s)}-\hat{g})$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}+\rho^{k+\alpha}\mathchoice{\left[{{{\rm D}^{(k)}_{\hat{g}}(g_{(s)}-\hat{g})}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}{[{{{\rm D}^{(k)}_{\hat{g}}(g_{(s)}-\hat{g})}}]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}{\left[{{{\rm D}^{(k)}_{\hat{g}}(g_{(s)}-\hat{g})}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}{\left[{{{\rm D}^{(k)}_{\hat{g}}(g_{(s)}-\hat{g})}}\right]_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha,\hat{g}$}}}}=o(1),

as
s\to+\infty
on
[R,+\infty)\times L
for every
R>0
.

Since
\omega^{*}_{s}{\,\rm d}\rho=r{\,\rm d}\rho
and
\omega^{*}_{s}{\,\rm d}\vartheta^{i}={\,\rm d}\vartheta^{i}
, is clear that
s^{-2}\omega^{*}_{s}\hat{g}=\hat{g}
. Thus the the case of
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical manifold follows from algebra operations on tensors. The result for
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\alpha\in[0,1]
follows in the same way from the fact that
{\rm D}_{\hat{g}}s^{-2}\omega^{*}_{s}g=s^{-2}\omega^{*}_{s}({\rm D}_{\hat{g}}g)
and
\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits_{\hat{g}}(x,y)=\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits_{\hat{g}}(\omega_{s}(x),\omega_{s}(y))/s
for every
x,y\in(1/s,+\infty)\times L
.∎

We want to highlight the relation between the coordinate
\rho
and the distance induced by
g
on
M
.

Lemma 2.15
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifold and
o\in M
. Then

(2.22)

\lim_{d_{g}(o,x)\to+\infty}\frac{d_{g}(o,x)}{\rho(x)}=1.

Observe that since
K
is compact there exist a
R>0
such that
d_{g}(o,x)>R
implies
x\in M\smallsetminus K
, hence (
2.22
) makes sense. Since
\pi
is a diffeomorphism, taking the limit for
d_{g}(o,x)\to+\infty
is the same of taking it for
\rho(x)\to+\infty
.

Since
\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|_{g}$}}=1+o(1)
, for every
\varepsilon>0
we can find
R_{\varepsilon}>1
such that
1-\varepsilon\leq\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|_{g}$}}\leq 1+\varepsilon
on
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\geq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\geq R_{\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}
. Pick
x\in\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\geq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\geq R_{\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}
and a curve
\gamma:[R_{\varepsilon},\rho(x)]\to M
which is the solution to the problem

(2.23)

\left\{\kern-3.0pt\begin{array}[]{l@{\ \ }l}\dot{\gamma}(s)=\frac{{\rm D}\rho}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|^{2}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|^{2}$}}{\raisebox{1.225pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.225pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|^{2}$}}{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-0.875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|^{2}$}}}(\gamma(s)),\\
\gamma(\rho(x))=x.\end{array}\right.

Computing the length of
\gamma
we get

(2.24)

\displaystyle L(\gamma)

\displaystyle=\int^{\rho(x)}_{R_{\varepsilon}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\dot{\gamma}(s)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\dot{\gamma}(s)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.4643pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.4643pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\dot{\gamma}(s)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.7143pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.7143pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\dot{\gamma}(s)$}}\right|$}}{\,\rm d}s=\int^{\rho(x)}_{R_{\varepsilon}}\frac{1}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|_{g}$}}}(\gamma(s)){\,\rm d}s\leq\frac{{\rho(x)}-R_{\varepsilon}}{1-\varepsilon},

which ensures that

(2.25)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\rho(x)\to+\infty}\frac{d_{g}(o,x)}{\rho(x)}

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\rho(x)\to+\infty}\frac{L(\gamma)+2\mathop{\rm diam}\nolimits(\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq R_{\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}})}{\rho(x)}\leq\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}.

Conversely, consider any geodesic
\sigma:[0,L]\to M
, parametrised by arc length, joining
\sigma(0)\in\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho=R_{\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho=R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}
and
\sigma(L)=x
. Then we obtain

(2.26)

\displaystyle\rho(x)-R_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{L}\mathchoice{\left\langle\vphantom{{\dot{\sigma}(s)}}{{\rm D}\rho}\kern 2.0pt\right|\left.\kern-1.0pt{\dot{\sigma}(s)}\vphantom{{{\rm D}\rho}}\right\rangle}{\langle{{\rm D}\rho}|{\dot{\sigma}(s)}\rangle}{\left\langle\vphantom{{\dot{\sigma}(s)}}{{\rm D}\rho}\kern 2.0pt\right|\left.\kern-5.0pt\vphantom{{{\rm D}\rho}}{\dot{\sigma}(s)}\right\rangle}{\left\langle\vphantom{{\dot{\sigma}(s)}}{{\rm D}\rho}\kern 2.0pt\right|\left.\kern-5.0pt{\dot{\sigma}(s)}\vphantom{{{\rm D}\rho}}\right\rangle}{\,\rm d}s\leq\int_{0}^{L}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|_{g}$}}(\sigma(s)){\,\rm d}s\leq(1+\varepsilon)L

which yields

(2.27)

\displaystyle\liminf_{\rho(x)\to+\infty}\frac{d_{g}(x,o)}{\rho(x)}

\displaystyle\geq\liminf_{\rho(x)\to+\infty}\frac{L-2\mathop{\rm diam}\nolimits(\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq R_{\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}})}{\rho(x)}

(2.28)

\displaystyle\geq\liminf_{\rho(x)\to+\infty}\frac{\rho(x)-R_{\varepsilon}-2\mathop{\rm diam}\nolimits(\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq R_{\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}})}{(1+{\varepsilon})\rho(x)}=\frac{1}{1+{\varepsilon}}.

By the arbitrariness of
\varepsilon>0
we can conclude.
∎

In Riemannian manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature, in virtue of Bishop-Gromov theorem, one can define an Asymptotic Volume Ratio since

(2.29)

\AVR(g)=\lim_{r\to+\infty}\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle B(o,R)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle B(o,R)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle B(o,R)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B(o,R)$}}\right|$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}R^{n}}

exists and does not depend on
o\in M
. Here we relaxed the condition on Ricci curvature so we can not apply Bishop-Gromov theorem, but on the other side we require an asymptotic behaviour for the metric, that allows to define an Asymptotic Volume Ratio as well.

Lemma 2.16
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifold. Then

(2.30)

\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle L$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle L$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle L$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle L$}}\right|$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}=\lim_{R\to+\infty}\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\{{1\leq\rho\leq R}\right\}}{\{{1\leq\rho\leq R}\}}{\left\{{1\leq\rho\leq R}\right\}}{\left\{{1\leq\rho\leq R}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{1\leq\rho\leq R}\right\}}{\{{1\leq\rho\leq R}\}}{\left\{{1\leq\rho\leq R}\right\}}{\left\{{1\leq\rho\leq R}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{1\leq\rho\leq R}\right\}}{\{{1\leq\rho\leq
