for every
\varphi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{c}(E\smallsetminus K_{\varepsilon})
. Moreover, for every
\mathrm{C}
there exists a function
\varphi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{c}(E\smallsetminus K_{\varepsilon})
such that

(2.45)

\left(\;\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{\frac{n}{n-1}}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{\frac{n}{n-1}}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{\frac{n}{n-1}}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{\frac{n}{n-1}}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{g}}\right)^{\frac{n-1}{n}}>\mathrm{C}\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{g}}

Then for every
\varepsilon<1
and
\mathrm{C}
we have
\varphi\in\mathrm{Lip}_{c}(E\smallsetminus K_{\varepsilon})
that satisfies

(2.46)

\displaystyle\left(\;\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{\frac{n}{n-1}}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{\frac{n}{n-1}}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{\frac{n}{n-1}}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{\frac{n}{n-1}}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{\hat{g}}}\right)^{\frac{n-1}{n}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{(1+\varepsilon)^{\frac{n-1}{n}}}\left(\;\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{\frac{n}{n-1}}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{\frac{n}{n-1}}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{\frac{n}{n-1}}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{\frac{n}{n-1}}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{g}}\right)^{\frac{n-1}{n}}>\frac{\mathrm{C}}{(1+\varepsilon)^{\frac{n-1}{n}}}\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{g}}

(2.47)

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{C}\,\frac{(1-\varepsilon)}{(1+\varepsilon)^{\frac{n-1}{n}}}\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{\hat{g}}}.

It is enough to choose
\varepsilon<1
and
\mathrm{C}
such that

(2.48)

\mathrm{C}\,\frac{(1-\varepsilon)}{(1+\varepsilon)^{\frac{n-1}{n}}}>\hat{\mathrm{C}}

to obtain a contradiction to the
L^{1}
-Sobolev Inequality on the asymptotic cone.∎

With some analogy, considering
\Omega\subseteq M
some open subset with smooth boundary and
u_{p}:M\smallsetminus\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
the
p
-capacitary potential associated to
\Omega
,
1<p<n
, we recall from the Introduction that

(2.49)

\mathrm{C}^{(i)}_{p}(\Omega)=\left(\frac{p-1}{n-p}\right)^{p-1}\frac{1}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}\int\limits_{\mathchoice{\left\{{u_{p}=1/t}\right\}}{\{{u_{p}=1/t}\}}{\left\{{u_{p}=1/t}\right\}}{\left\{{u_{p}=1/t}\right\}}\cap E_{i}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.31946pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.31946pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{2.31946pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.31946pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{1.6389pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.6389pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{1.0139pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.0139pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\,\rm d}\sigma=\frac{\mathrm{C}_{p}\left(\mathchoice{\left\{{u\leq\frac{1}{t}}\right\}}{\{{u\leq\frac{1}{t}}\}}{\left\{{u\leq\frac{1}{t}}\right\}}{\left\{{u\leq\frac{1}{t}}\right\}}\cap E_{i};E_{i}\right)}{t^{p-1}}.

This is well defined since for every
T\in[1,+\infty)
large enough,
\mathchoice{\left\{{u_{p}>1/T}\right\}}{\{{u_{p}>1/T}\}}{\left\{{u_{p}>1/T}\right\}}{\left\{{u_{p}>1/T}\right\}}
contains the compact
K
in
definition

2.13
and the quantity in
eq.

2.49
does not depend on
t\geq T
. Moreover, it is readily checked that, by (
2.10
), the
p
-capacity of
\Omega
splits as

(2.50)

\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathrm{C}^{(i)}_{p}(\Omega).

The quantity
\mathrm{C}^{(i)}_{p}(\Omega)
represents the portion of
\Omega
that contributes to its
p
-capacity under the influence of the end
E_{i}
. In the case
\Omega
is already contains
K
in
definition

2.17
it is exactly the
p
-capacity of
\Omega\cap E_{i}
.

On cones, the
p
-capacity of the cross section
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}{\{{\rho=r}\}}{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}
can be easily computed since the function
u=(\rho/r)^{-(n-p)/(p-1)}
is the
p
-capacitary potential associated to these sets. In Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifold one might expect that the
p
-capacity of
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}{\{{\rho=r}\}}{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}
approaches the model one for large
r
. Despite the definition of the
p
-capacity involves the first order derivatives of the
p
-capacitary potential, the convergence is also true even if the metric converges only in the
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-topology.

Lemma 2.21
.

Let
(M,g)
be a
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifold. Let
\rho
be the radial coordinate. Then

(2.51)

\lim_{r\to+\infty}\frac{{\rm Cap}_{p}\left(\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq r}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}\right)}{r^{n-p}\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}=\left(\frac{n-p}{p-1}\right)^{p-1}\AVR(g).

Since the metric
g
converges to the metric
\hat{g}
, for every
\varepsilon>0
there exists a
R_{\varepsilon}>0
such that for every
r\geq R_{\varepsilon}

(2.52)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.38611pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-0.38611pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\;\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{g}}-\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{\hat{g}}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{0.38611pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-0.38611pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\;\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{g}}-\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{\hat{g}}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{-0.19456pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{0.19456pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\;\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.225pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.225pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-0.875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{g}}-\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.225pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.225pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-0.875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{\hat{g}}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{-1.30554pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{1.30554pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\;\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-0.875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{0.625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-0.625pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{g}}-\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-0.875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{0.625pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-0.625pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{\hat{g}}}$}}\right|$}}\leq\varepsilon\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{\hat{g}}}

holds for every function
\varphi\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\geq r}\right\}}{\{{\rho\geq r}\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq r}\right\}})
such that
\varphi=1
on
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}{\{{\rho=r}\}}{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}
. In particular, we have that

(2.53)

(1-\varepsilon)\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{\hat{g}}}\leq\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{g}^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{g}}\leq(1+\varepsilon)\int\limits_{M}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\varphi$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}^{p}$}}{\,\rm d}{\mu_{\hat{g}}}.

The set
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\geq r}\right\}}{\{{\rho\geq r}\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq r}\right\}}
is diffeomorphic to
[r,+\infty)\times L
where
L
is the cross section of the cone
(M,g)
is asymptotic to. Hence, the family of
\varphi
considered above are in one-to-one correspondence with the competitors for the
p
-capacity of
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq r}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}
in the Riemannian cone
[r,+\infty)\times L
. Dividing each side by
\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|
, recalling the characterisation of
\AVR(g)
in
definition

2.17
and taking the infimum on each side of the previous chain of inequalities we are left with

(2.54)

(1-\varepsilon)r^{n-p}\AVR(g)\left(\frac{n-p}{p-1}\right)^{p-1}\leq\frac{{\rm Cap}_{p}(\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq r}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}})}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}\leq(1+\varepsilon)r^{n-p}\AVR(g)\left(\frac{n-p}{p-1}\right)^{p-1}.

dividing each term by
r^{n-p}
and sending to the limit as
r\to+\infty
we have that

(2.55)

(1-\varepsilon)\AVR(g)\left(\frac{n-p}{p-1}\right)^{p-1}\leq\lim_{r\to+\infty}\frac{{\rm Cap}_{p}(\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq r}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}})}{r^{n-p}\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}\leq(1+\varepsilon)\AVR(g)\left(\frac{n-p}{p-1}\right)^{p-1}

which in turns gives (
2.51
) by arbitrariness of
\varepsilon
. ∎

2.4.
Li-Yau-type estimates and gradient bound

We will provide Li-Yau-type estimates for the
p
-Green function
G_{p}
with a controlled constant as
p\to 1^{+}
. These estimates will be the starting point for the proof of both
theorems

1.1
and
1.2
. We highlight that in
[
MRS21
]
the authors provided Li-Yau-type estimates for
p
-harmonic functions. The upper bound in
[
MRS21
, Theorem 3.6]
carried out in a broader setting is actually in term of the distance. Conversely, the lower bound in
[
MRS21
, Corollary 2.8]
is in terms of the distance in a model which has the same radial sectional curvature of the lower bound in
eq.

1.3
.
This estimate does not seem sufficient for our aims.

However, since our setting disposes of a precise asymptotic structure we can improve such lower bound by inheriting some techniques coming from
[
Hol99
]
, and using the natural foliaton of ends induced by the cross-sections of the asymptotic cone. To accomplish this program we first need a global Harnack Inequality holding on each end of
(M,g)
.

Proposition 2.22

(Harnack’s Inequality)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifold with Ricci curvature satisfying (
1.3
) and let
p>1
. A uniform Harnack Inequality holds on every
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=R}\right\}}{\{{\rho=R}\}}{\left\{{\rho=R}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=R}\right\}}\cap E
, that is there exists a constant
\mathrm{C}_{H}>0
that depends only on the dimension and
p
but not on
R
such that

(2.56)

\inf_{\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=R}\right\}}{\{{\rho=R}\}}{\left\{{\rho=R}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=R}\right\}}\cap E}u\geq\mathrm{C}_{H}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\sup_{\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=R}\right\}}{\{{\rho=R}\}}{\left\{{\rho=R}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=R}\right\}}\cap E}u

for every positive
p
-harmonic function
u
on
E
. Moreover,
\mathrm{C}_{H}
is bounded as
p\to 1^{+}

proposition

2.20
and
[
Hol99
, Example 2.20]
guarantees that the hypotheses of
[
MRS21
, Theorem 3.4]
are satisfied on
B(x,6r)
for every
x\in M\smallsetminus B(o,2R)
. Hence we have that

(2.57)

\sup_{B(x,R)}u\leq\mathrm{C}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\inf_{B(x,R)}u

for every positive
p
-harmonic function
u
on
E
, for some constant which is bounded for large
R
and in
p
as
p\to 1^{+}
. Since the
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=R}\right\}}{\{{\rho=R}\}}{\left\{{\rho=R}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=R}\right\}}\cap E
is connected and its diameter increases linearly in
R
by the asymptotic assumption, it can be covered with
N
balls of radius
R
, for some
N
not depending on
R
. By chaining
eq.

2.57

N
times we obtain
eq.

2.56
. ∎

As a consequence we can overcome the main issue in
[
Hol99
, Proposition 5.9]
, that is a control on the bounded components of
E\smallsetminus B(o,r)
. The proof of
[
Hol99
, Proposition 5.9]
suggest also an explicit value for the constant.

Proposition 2.23
.

Let
(M,g)
be a
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifold with Ricci curvature satisfying (
1.3
) and
o\in M
. For every end
E
of
M
there exists a constant
\mathrm{C}>0
such that

(2.58)

\displaystyle\sup_{\partial E(r)}G(o,x)

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{C}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\int\limits_{2r}^{+\infty}\left(\frac{t}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle B(o,t)\cap E$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle B(o,t)\cap E$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle B(o,t)\cap E$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B(o,t)\cap E$}}\right|$}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}{\,\rm d}t

holds for every
r>0
where
E(r)
is the unbounded component of
E\smallsetminus\overline{B(o,r)}
. Moreover, the constant
\mathrm{C}=a\,{\rm Cap}_{p}(\overline{K};E)
where
a>0
is such that
G_{p}(x,o)\geq a^{1/(p-1)}
on
M\smallsetminus K
and
K
is the compact set in
definition

2.13
.

We already observed that
(M,g)
as a finite number of ends with connected boundary in
lemma

2.18
. Denote by
E
one of them. Since
E
is
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical, the volume of
B(o,r)\cap E
grows like
r^{n}
, then for some large
R>0

(2.59)

\int\limits_{R}^{+\infty}\left(\frac{r}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle B(o,r)\cap E$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle B(o,r)\cap E$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle B(o,r)\cap E$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B(o,r)\cap E$}}\right|$}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}{\,\rm d}r\leq\mathrm{C}R^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}<+\infty

that is
E
is
p
-large.
E
satisfies a weak
(1,p)
-Poincaré Inequality and a volume-doubling property (see
[
Hol99
, Example 2.20]
. Moreover,
E
satisfies the volume comparison condition which means that there exists a constant
\mathrm{C}_{v}
such that

(2.60)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle E\cap B(o,r)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle E\cap B(o,r)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle E\cap B(o,r)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle E\cap B(o,r)$}}\right|$}}\leq\mathrm{C}_{v}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle B(x,r/8)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle B(x,r/8)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle B(x,r/8)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B(x,r/8)$}}\right|$}}

holds for any
r\geq R
and
x\in\partial B(o,r)\cap E
. Observe that (
2.60
) holds on the cones. Indeed, since
L
is compact
\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle B(x,r/8)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle B(x,r/8)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle B(x,r/8)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B(x,r/8)$}}\right|$}}\geq\mu r^{n}/8^{n}
for some positive
\mu>0
. If
E
is merely
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical, both the volume of
E\cap B(o,r)
and
B(x,r/8)
are approaching the corresponding ones on the cone, which proves (
2.60
).

With the very same arguments as in
[
Hol99
, Proposition 5.9]
one can prove (
2.58
), by restricting all quantities to the given end. A close look to
[
Hol99
, Proposition 5.9]
gives also that the constant can be chosen as above, firstly choosing
a>0
and following the computations accordingly. ∎

The above Proposition implies the following double-sided bound on
G_{p}
, that will in turn imply an analogous one for
u_{p}
. Such double bound will be key for working out the asymptotics.

Theorem 2.24

(Li-Yau-type estimates)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifold with
\Ric
satisfying (
1.3
). Let
p>1
, each end of
M
is
p
-nonparabolic and, given
G_{p}
the
p
-Green function, then there exist two constants
\mathrm{C}_{L},\mathrm{C}_{U}>0

(2.61)

\mathrm{C}_{L}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}d_{g}(o,x)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}\leq G_{p}(o,x)\leq\mathrm{C}_{U}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}d_{g}(o,x)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}.

for every
x\in M\smallsetminus\{o\}
. Moreover, the constant
\mathrm{C}_{U}
is bounded as
p\to 1^{+}
and there exists
R>0
not depending on
p
such that the lower bound in
eq.

2.61
holds with constant
\mathrm{C}_{L}=\mathrm{C}\,a\,{\rm Cap}_{p}(\overline{K};E)
on
M\smallsetminus B(o,R)
, where
a>0
is such that
G_{p}(x,o)\geq a^{1/(p-1)}
on
M\smallsetminus K
,
\mathrm{C}
does not depend on
p
and
K
is the bounded set in
definition

2.13
.

The upper bound in
eq.

2.61
with
\mathrm{C}_{U}
bounded as
p\to 1^{+}
follows from
[
MRS21
, Theorem 3.6]
. Indeed, the assumptions are satisfied since a
p
-Sobolev Inequality holds true as a standard consequence of the
L^{1}
-Sobolev inequality in
proposition

2.20
.
For what it concerns the lower bound, we are in position to apply
proposition

2.23
. The main issue is that we do not have control on the bounded components of
E\smallsetminus\overline{B(o,R)}
. Consider the function
R:[1,+\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
defined as

(2.62)

R(t)=\max\mathchoice{\left\{{d_{g}(o,x)\left.\vphantom{d_{g}(o,x)x\in\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\{{\rho=t}\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}}\right|x\in\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\{{\rho=t}\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}}\right\}}{\{{d_{g}(o,x)\left.\vphantom{d_{g}(o,x)x\in\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\{{\rho=t}\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}}\right|x\in\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\{{\rho=t}\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}}\}}{\left\{{d_{g}(o,x)\left.\vphantom{d_{g}(o,x)x\in\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\{{\rho=t}\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}}\right|x\in\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\{{\rho=t}\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}}\right\}}{\left\{{d_{g}(o,x)\left.\vphantom{d_{g}(o,x)x\in\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\{{\rho=t}\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}}\right|x\in\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\{{\rho=t}\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}}\right\}}.

Observe that
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\geq t}\right\}}{\{{\rho\geq t}\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq t}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq t}\right\}}\supset E(2R(t))
. Then applying the Harnack’s Inequality (
2.56
), Comparison Principle and (
2.58
) one gets that

(2.63)

\displaystyle\inf_{\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\{{\rho=t}\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}}G_{p}(o,x)

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{C}_{H}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\sup_{\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\{{\rho=t}\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=t}\right\}}}G_{p}(o,x)\geq\mathrm{C}_{H}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\sup_{\partial E(2R(t))}G_{p}(o,x)

(2.64)

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{C}_{4}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\int\limits_{4R(t)}^{+\infty}\left(\frac{r}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle B(o,r)\cap E$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle B(o,r)\cap E$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle B(o,r)\cap E$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle B(o,r)\cap E$}}\right|$}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}{\,\rm d}r\geq\mathrm{C}_{5}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}R(t)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}.

for every
t\geq T
, for some
T
large enough not depending on
p
and
\mathrm{C}_{5}=\mathrm{C}a{\rm Cap}_{p}(\partial K;E)
. By
lemma

2.15
there exists
R_{1}\geq R(T)
such that
\partial B(o,r)\subset\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq 2r}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq 2r}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq 2r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq 2r}\right\}}
and
R(2r)\leq 4r
hold for every
r\geq R_{1}
. Then, by the Maximum Principle,

(2.65)

\inf_{\partial B(o,r)}G_{p}(o,x)\geq\inf_{\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=2r}\right\}}{\{{\rho=2r}\}}{\left\{{\rho=2r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=2r}\right\}}}G_{p}(o,x)\geq\mathrm{C}_{5}^{\frac{1}{p-1}}R(2r)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}\geq\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}_{5}}{4^{n-p}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}r^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}},

holds for every
r\geq 1
, since
R_{1}
does not depend on
p
. The global lower bound follows since it is satisfied near the pole
o
, but the new constant
\mathrm{C}_{L}
might go to
0
as
p\to 1^{+}
∎

Arguing as done in the proof of
[
BFM21
, Theorem 2.15]
, it is easy to derive analogous estimates for
u_{p}
. We do not take trace of the best constant as in the previous result, since this tool will be only used to derive
theorem

1.1
where
1<p<n
is fixed.

Corollary 2.25

(Li-Yau-type estimates for the
p
-capacitary potential)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifold with
\Ric
satisfying (
1.3
). Let be
1<p<n
. There exists a unique solution
u_{p}
to (
1.2
) and there exists a positive and finite constant
\mathrm{C}
such that

(2.66)

\mathrm{C}^{-1}d_{g}(x,o)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}\leq u_{p}(x)\leq\mathrm{C}d_{g}(x,o)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}

for every
x\in M\smallsetminus\Omega
.

The existence follows from (
2.61
) and
theorem

2.9
. Moreover, In light of (
2.61
), it suffices to show that there exists a positive finite constant
\mathrm{C}
such that
\mathrm{C}^{-1}G_{p}\leq u_{p}\leq\mathrm{C}G_{p}
. Choose any
\sup_{\partial\Omega}u_{p}<\mathrm{C}
. Then,
\mathrm{C}^{-1}G_{p}<u_{p}
on
\partial\Omega
. Moreover, since both
u_{p}
and
G_{p}
vanish at infinity, for any
\delta>0
we have
\mathrm{C}^{-1}G_{p}<u_{p}+\delta
on
\partial B(o,R)
for any
R
big enough. The Comparison Principle applied to the
p
-harmonic functions
u_{p}+\delta
and
G_{p}
in
B(o,R)\smallsetminus\overline{\Omega}
shows that
\mathrm{C}^{-1}G_{p}<u_{p}+\delta
in the latter subset. The radius
R
being arbitrarily big, this implies that, by passing to the limit as
R\to+\infty
, that
\mathrm{C}^{-1}G_{p}<u_{p}+\delta
in the whole
M\smallsetminus\Omega
. Letting
\delta\to 0^{+}
leaves with
\mathrm{C}^{-1}G_{p}\leq u_{p}
, and consequently with the lower bound in (
2.66
). The inequality
u_{p}\leq\mathrm{C}G_{p}
, yielding the upper bound, is shown the same way. ∎

We recall the following Cheng-Yau-type estimate, proved in
[
WZ10
]
, together with the consequent gradient bound for the
p
-capacitary potential.

Theorem 2.26

(Cheng-Yau-type estimate)
.

Let
(M,g)
a complete Riemannian manifold. Let
v\in W^{1,p}_{\mathrm{loc}}B(o,2R)
be a positive
p
-harmonic function on a geodesic ball
B(o,2R)
for some
R>0
where
\Ric\geq-(n-1)\kappa^{2}
. Then there exists a constant
\mathrm{C}=\mathrm{C}(p,n)
such that

(2.67)

\sup_{B(o,R)}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\log(v)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\log(v)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\log(v)$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\log(v)$}}\right|$}}\leq\mathrm{C}\left(\frac{1}{R}+\kappa\right).

The above local estimate enables us to provide a global gradient bound for
u_{p}
, that will be key for its asymptotic analysis.

Proposition 2.27
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical manifold with
\Ric
satisfying (
1.3
). Let
\Omega\subset M
open bounded with smooth boundary and let
u_{p}
be its
p
-capacitary potential. Then, there exists a constant
\mathrm{C}>0
such that

(2.68)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.31946pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.31946pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\log u_{p}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.31946pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.31946pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\log u_{p}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.6389pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.6389pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\log u_{p}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.0139pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.0139pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\log u_{p}$}}\right|$}}\leq\frac{\mathrm{C}}{\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(x,o)}

holds on the whole
M\smallsetminus\Omega
.

By the
\mathscr{C}^{1}
-regularity of
u
, it clearly suffices to show that (
2.68
) holds true outside some compact set containing
{\Omega}
. Let then
o\in\Omega
and
R>0
be such that
\Omega\subset B(o,R)
, and let
x\in M\smallsetminus\overline{B(o,2R)}
. With this choice, we have
B(x,\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(o,x)-R)\subset M\smallsetminus\overline{B(o,R)}
. Thus, applying inequality (
2.67
) to the function
u_{p}
in the ball
B(x,\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(o,x)-R)
we get

(2.69)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.31946pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.31946pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.31946pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.31946pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.6389pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.6389pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.0139pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.0139pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}u_{p}$}}\right|$}}}{u_{p}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathrm{C}\left(\frac{1}{\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(o,x)-R}+\frac{\kappa}{\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(o,x)+1}\right)\leq 2\mathrm{C}\frac{(2+\kappa)}{\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(o,x)}.

concluding the proof.
∎

The constant in
eq.

2.67
and consequently in
eq.

2.68
is such that
(p-1)\mathrm{C}
diverges as
p\to 1^{+}
. Up to the authors’ knowledge a Cheng-Yau-type estimate with
(p-1)\mathrm{C}
controlled in
p
is yet to be discovered. This should lead to various other insights about the weak IMCF and its relations with
p
-harmonic potentials.

3.
Asymptotic behaviour of the
p
-capacitary potential

In this section we prove
theorem

1.1
. In fact, as anticipated in the Introduction, we prove a more general statement that provides information also about the asymptotic behaviour of the derivatives of
u
, if the asymptotic structure of the underlying metric is suitably reinforced.

Theorem 3.1

(Asymptotic behaviour of the derivatives of
p
-capacitary potential)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a complete
\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}
-Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifold for some
\alpha>0
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
with
\Ric
satisfying
eq.

1.3
. Let
E_{1},\ldots,E_{N}
be the (finitely many) ends of
M
with respect to the compact
K
in
definition

2.13
. Consider
\Omega\subset M
be an open bounded subset with smooth boundary and
u:M\smallsetminus\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
a solution of the problem (
1.2
). Then

(3.1)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.06447pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.06447pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}^{\ell}u-\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}^{(i)}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g;E_{i})}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}{\rm D}^{\ell}\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{2.99501pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.99501pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}^{\ell}u-\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}^{(i)}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g;E_{i})}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}{\rm D}^{\ell}\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{2.54166pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.54166pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}^{\ell}u-\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}^{(i)}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g;E_{i})}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}{\rm D}^{\ell}\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{2.55556pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.55556pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}^{\ell}u-\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}^{(i)}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g;E_{i})}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}{\rm D}^{\ell}\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}=o\left(\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}-\ell}\right)

on
E_{i}
as
\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(o,x)\to+\infty
for every
i=1,\ldots,N
and
\ell\leq k+1
.

For future reference we want to specify the behaviour of partial derivatives coming from
eq.

3.1
. Given a coordinate system
(\vartheta^{1},\ldots,\vartheta^{n-1})
on
L
one has that

(3.2)

\frac{\partial^{j+\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\alpha$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\alpha$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.225pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.225pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-0.875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}\right|$}}}u}{\partial\rho^{j}\partial\vartheta^{\alpha}}=\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}^{(i)}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g;E_{i})}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\frac{\partial^{j+\mathchoice{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\alpha$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\alpha$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.225pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.225pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{0.875pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-0.875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}\right|$}}}}{\partial\rho^{j}\partial\vartheta^{\alpha}}\left(\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}\right)+o\left(\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}-j}\right)

as
\rho\to+\infty
, where
\alpha
is a
(n-1)
-dimensional multi-index such that
j+\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\alpha$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\alpha$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\alpha$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$}}\right|$}}\leq k
.

Along with the proof, we extend
lemma

2.21
, showing that the
p
-capacity of the
p
-capacitary potential behaves like the
p
-capacity of the cross sections approaching infinity.

Proposition 3.2

(Asymptotic behaviour of the
p
-capacity of level sets)
.

In the same assumptions and notations of
theorem

1.1
, set, for
i=1,\dots,N
,

(3.3)

v_{i}=\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}^{(i)}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g;E_{i})}\right)^{\frac{1}{n-p}}u^{-\frac{p-1}{n-p}}.

Then, we have

(3.4)

\lim_{s\to+\infty}\frac{{\rm Cap}_{p}(\mathchoice{\left\{{v_{i}\leq s}\right\}}{\{{v_{i}\leq s}\}}{\left\{{v_{i}\leq s}\right\}}{\left\{{v_{i}\leq s}\right\}}\cap E_{i};E_{i})}{s^{n-p}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-p}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-p}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-p}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-p}$}}\right|$}}}=\left(\frac{n-p}{p-1}\right)^{p-1}\AVR(g;E_{i}).

Moreover, as a byproduct, we obtain the following uniqueness result on Riemannian cones.

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
((0,+\infty)\times L,\hat{g})
be a Riemannian cone with nonnegative Ricci curvature where
L
is a closed connected smooth hypersurface. Let
u
be a nonnegative
p
-harmonic function on
(0,+\infty)\times L
satisfying
u(x)\leq\mathrm{C}\rho(x)^{-(n-p)/(p-1)}
for every
x\in(0,+\infty)\times L
for some constant
\mathrm{C}\geq 0
. Then, there exists a nonnegative
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
such that

(3.5)

u(x)=\gamma\rho(x)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}

holds on
(0,+\infty)\times L
.

It is enough to prove the theorems in the case
M
has only one end. The proof of the general case then follows applying the result to each end. We will denote by
g_{(s)}
the metric
s^{-2}\omega_{s}^{*}g
on
[1/s,+\infty)\times L
, being
\omega_{s}
the family of diffeomorphisms defined in
eq.

2.20
. We find convenient to organise the proof in four steps. The first three steps are devoted do prove
theorem

1.1
. The second and the third one contain the proof of
proposition

3.3
and
proposition

3.2
respectively. In the last we complete the proof for higher order asymptotic behaviour mainly using Schauder estimates for
p
-harmonic functions.

Suppose that
(M,g)
is
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical. Define the family of functions
u_{s}:[1/s,+\infty)\times L\to\mathbb{R}
as

(3.6)

u_{s}(x)=s^{\frac{n-p}{p-1}}u\circ\omega_{s}(x)

where
\omega_{s}
is the map defined in
eq.

2.20
. The aim of this step is to prove compactness of
(u_{s})_{s\geq 1}
with respect to local uniform convergence on
(0,+\infty)\times L
. In particular, by
theorem

2.5
(see also
remark

2.6
) any limit point
w
for the sequence
(u_{s})_{s\geq 1}
is
p
-harmonic with respect to the metric
\hat{g}
on
(0,+\infty)\times L
. Moreover, there exists a positive constant
\mathrm{C}
such that

(3.7)

\mathrm{C}^{-1}\rho(x)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}\leq w(x)\leq\mathrm{C}\rho(x)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}

is satisfied for every
x\in(0,+\infty)\times L
.

By
corollary

2.25
we have that

(3.8)

\mathrm{C}_{1}^{-1}\left(\vphantom{\sum}\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(o,x)\right)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}\leq u(x)\leq\mathrm{C}_{1}\left(\vphantom{\sum}\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(o,x)\right)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}.

holds on
M\smallsetminus\Omega
. In particular, since by
lemma

2.15
the distance function from
o
behaves asymptotically as the coordinate
\rho
, we deduce that there exist
S_{2},\mathrm{C}_{2}>0
such that

(3.9)

\mathrm{C}_{2}^{-1}\rho(x)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}\leq u_{s}(x)\leq\mathrm{C}_{2}\rho(x)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}},

holds on
[1/s,+\infty)\times L
for every
s\geq S_{2}
. In particular,
(u_{s})_{s\geq 1}
is equibounded.
By the gradient estimate
proposition

2.27

(3.10)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}u$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}u$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}u$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}u$}}\right|$}}(x)\leq\mathrm{C}_{3}u(x)^{\frac{n-1}{n-p}}\leq\mathrm{C}_{4}\left(\vphantom{\sum}\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(o,x)\right)^{-\frac{n-1}{p-1}}

for some positive constants
\mathrm{C}_{3},\mathrm{C}_{4}
. Hence, employing again
lemma

2.15
there exist
S_{5},\mathrm{C}_{5}>0
such that

(3.11)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}u_{s}$}}\right|_{g_{(s)}}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}u_{s}$}}\right|_{g_{(s)}}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}u_{s}$}}\right|_{g_{(s)}}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}u_{s}$}}\right|_{g_{(s)}}$}}(x)\leq\mathrm{C}_{5}\rho(x)^{-\frac{n-1}{p-1}}

holds on
[1/s,+\infty)\times L
for every
s\geq S_{5}
. By
lemma

2.14
we have that for some
\varepsilon>0
there is
S_{6}>0
such that

(3.12)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}u_{s}$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}u_{s}$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}u_{s}$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}u_{s}$}}\right|_{\hat{g}}$}}\leq(1+\varepsilon)\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}u_{s}$}}\right|_{g_{(s)}}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}u_{s}$}}\right|_{g_{(s)}}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}u_{s}$}}\right|_{g_{(s)}}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}u_{s}$}}\right|_{g_{(s)}}$}}

holds for every
s\geq S_{6}
. Combining it with
eq.

3.11
we obtain that the family
(u_{s})_{s\geq 1}
is equicontinuous. By Arzelà-Ascoli Theorem we conclude that
(u_{s})_{s\geq 1}
is precompact with respect to the local uniform convergence.
eq.

3.7
follows from
eq.

3.9
.

Here we prove that any limit point
v
of the family
(u_{s})_{s\geq 1}
has the form

(3.13)

v(x)=\gamma\rho(x)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}},

for some nonnegative
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
, proving also
proposition

3.3
.
Let
v:(0,+\infty)\times L\to\mathbb{R}
be a nonnegative
p
-harmonic function satisfying the bound
v(x)\leq\mathrm{C}\rho(x)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}
on
(0,+\infty)\times L
.

Define the function
\mathfrak{e}_{v}:(0,+\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
as

(3.14)

\mathfrak{e}_{v}(t)=\frac{R(t)}{r(t)},

where
[r(t),R(t)]\times L
is the smallest annulus containing
\mathchoice{\left\{{v=1/t}\right\}}{\{{v=1/t}\}}{\left\{{v=1/t}\right\}}{\left\{{v=1/t}\right\}}
for every
t\in(0,+\infty)
.
Observe that,
\mathfrak{e}_{v}(t)\geq 1
and
\mathfrak{e}_{v}(t)=1
if and only if
\mathchoice{\left\{{v=1/t}\right\}}{\{{v=1/t}\}}{\left\{{v=1/t}\right\}}{\left\{{v=1/t}\right\}}
is a cross-section of the cone. By the Comparison Principle
theorem

2.4
, using the potentials of
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=r(t)}\right\}}{\{{\rho=r(t)}\}}{\left\{{\rho=r(t)}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=r(t)}\right\}}
and
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=R(t)}\right\}}{\{{\rho=R(t)}\}}{\left\{{\rho=R(t)}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=R(t)}\right\}}
as barriers, we have that

(3.15)

r(t)\left(\frac{t}{T}\right)^{\frac{n-p}{p-1}}\leq\rho(x)\leq R(t)\left(\frac{t}{T}\right)^{\frac{n-p}{p-1}}

holds for every
x\in\mathchoice{\left\{{v=1/T}\right\}}{\{{v=1/T}\}}{\left\{{v=1/T}\right\}}{\left\{{v=1/T}\right\}}
for every
T\geq t
. Hence,
\mathfrak{e}_{v}
is nonincreasing. Moreover, since
(0,+\infty)\times L
is connected,
\rho(x)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}
is
p
-harmonic and
\mathscr{C}^{2}((0,+\infty)\times L)
and
\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.74132pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.74132pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}$}}\right|$}}\geq\frac{n-p}{p-1}R^{-\frac{n-1}{p-1}}
holds on
(0,R)\times L
for every
R>0
, by the Strong Comparison Principle
theorem

2.4
the inequalities in
eq.

3.15
are strict unless
\mathchoice{\left\{{v=t}\right\}}{\{{v=t}\}}{\left\{{v=t}\right\}}{\left\{{v=t}\right\}}
is a cross-section. It is not hard to see that
\mathfrak{e}_{v}
is scale invariant

Consider for
s\leq 1
the family
v_{s}:[1,+\infty)\times L\to\mathbb{R}
defined as

(3.16)

v_{s}(x)=s^{\frac{n-p}{p-1}}v\circ\omega_{s}(x)

where
\omega_{s}
is defined in (
2.20
).
Using the same argument of
section

3
we have that

(3.17)

\displaystyle v_{s}(x)\leq\mathrm{C}\rho(x)^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}

and

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v_{s}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v_{s}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v_{s}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v_{s}$}}\right|$}}(x)\leq\mathrm{C}\rho(x)^{-\frac{n-1}{p-1}}

holds on
(0,+\infty)\times L
for some constant
\mathrm{C}>0
. Hence, appealing to the Arzelà-Ascoli Theorem,
(v_{s})_{s\leq 1}
is precompact with respect to the local uniform convergence. Let
w
be a limit point for
(v_{s})_{s\leq 1}
. Since
\mathfrak{e}
is scale invariant,
\mathfrak{e}_{v_{s}}(t)=\mathfrak{e}_{v}(t/s)
. Then,
\mathfrak{e}_{w}(t)
is constant equal to some
\mathfrak{e}_{w}\in[1,+\infty)
that by monotonicity satisfies
\mathfrak{e}_{w}=\sup_{t}\mathfrak{e}_{v}(t)\in[1,+\infty)
. Suppose by contradiction that
\mathfrak{e}_{w}>1
. Then the level
\mathchoice{\left\{{w=1}\right\}}{\{{w=1}\}}{\left\{{w=1}\right\}}{\left\{{w=1}\right\}}\subset[r(1),\mathfrak{e}_{w}r(1)]\times L
and
\mathchoice{\left\{{w=1}\right\}}{\{{w=1}\}}{\left\{{w=1}\right\}}{\left\{{w=1}\right\}}
touches both the cross-sections
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=r(1)}\right\}}{\{{\rho=r(1)}\}}{\left\{{\rho=r(1)}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=r(1)}\right\}}
and
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=\mathfrak{e}_{w}r(1)}\right\}}{\{{\rho=\mathfrak{e}_{w}r(1)}\}}{\left\{{\rho=\mathfrak{e}_{w}r(1)}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=\mathfrak{e}_{w}r(1)}\right\}}
without being equal to either one. By
eq.

3.15
and the Strong Comparison Principle
theorem

2.4

(3.18)

r(1)t^{\frac{p-1}{n-p}}<\rho(x)<\mathfrak{e}_{w}r(1)t^{\frac{p-1}{n-p}}

holds for every
x\in\mathchoice{\left\{{w=1/t}\right\}}{\{{w=1/t}\}}{\left\{{w=1/t}\right\}}{\left\{{w=1/t}\right\}}
for every
t>1
. We therefore have that
\mathfrak{e}_{w}(t)<\mathfrak{e}_{w}
which is a contradiction. In conclusion
\mathfrak{e}_{w}
must be
1
and since
1\leq\mathfrak{e}_{v}(t)\leq\mathfrak{e}_{w}=1
,
v
is as in (
3.13
).

By
section

3
, any limit point
w
for the sequence
(u_{s})_{s\geq 1}
given by
section

3
has the form
\gamma\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}
, where
\gamma>0
by (
3.7
). We are now going to prove that

(3.19)

\gamma=\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\AVR(g)^{-\frac{1}{p-1}}.

The characterisation (
3.19
) ensures that any converging subsequence admits the same limit, proving that the whole family
(u_{s})_{s\geq 1}
locally uniformly converges to
\gamma\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}
as
s\to+\infty
. In particular, for every
\varepsilon>0
there exists a
S\geq 1
such that

\begin{split}\sup_{\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=s}\right\}}{\{{\rho=s}\}}{\left\{{\rho=s}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=s}\right\}}}s^{\frac{n-p}{p-1}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.31946pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.31946pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle u-\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle u-\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.54166pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.54166pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle u-\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.55556pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.55556pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u-\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}$}}\right|$}}&=\sup_{\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=1}\right\}}{\{{\rho=1}\}}{\left\{{\rho=1}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=1}\right\}}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.31946pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.31946pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle u_{s}-\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle u_{s}-\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.54166pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.54166pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle u_{s}-\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.55556pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.55556pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle u_{s}-\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}$}}\right|$}}\leq\varepsilon\end{split}

for every
s\geq S
, proving
theorems

1.1
and
3.2
.

Observe that
\gamma>0
by
corollary

2.25
. In order to prove (
3.19
), we find convenient to work with the auxiliary function

(3.20)

v=\left(\frac{u}{\gamma}\right)^{-\frac{p-1}{n-p}}.

Since
w
is a limit point for the family
(u_{s})_{s\geq 1}
there is a subsequence
(u_{s_{k}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
,
s_{k}
increasing and divergent as
k\to+\infty
, such that
u_{s_{k}}\to v=\gamma\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}
locally uniformly on
(0,+\infty)\times L
as
k\to+\infty
. Then for any
\varepsilon>0
there exists
k_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(3.21)

\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}\subset\mathchoice{\left\{{v\leq s_{k}}\right\}}{\{{v\leq s_{k}}\}}{\left\{{v\leq s_{k}}\right\}}{\left\{{v\leq s_{k}}\right\}}\subset\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}

By the monotonicity of the
p
-capacity with respect to the inclusion, we have that

(3.22)

{\rm Cap}_{p}\left(\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}\right)\leq{\rm Cap}_{p}\left(\mathchoice{\left\{{v\leq s_{k}}\right\}}{\{{v\leq s_{k}}\}}{\left\{{v\leq s_{k}}\right\}}{\left\{{v\leq s_{k}}\right\}}\right)\leq{\rm Cap}_{p}\left(\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}\right)

By
eq.

2.12
we can compute the capacity of level sets of
v
in terms of the capacity of
\partial\Omega
, that is

(3.23)

{\rm Cap}_{p}\left(\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}\right)\leq\gamma^{-(p-1)}s_{k}^{n-p}{\rm Cap}_{p}\left(\Omega\right)\leq{\rm Cap}_{p}\left(\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}\right)

Dividing each side by
\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}s_{k}^{n-p}
, sending
k\to+\infty
and using
lemma

2.21
we infer that

(3.24)

\left(\frac{n-p}{p-1}\right)^{p-1}\frac{\AVR(g)}{(1+\varepsilon)^{n-p}}\leq\gamma^{-(p-1)}\frac{{\rm Cap}_{p}(\Omega)}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}\leq\left(\frac{n-p}{p-1}\right)^{p-1}\frac{\AVR(g)}{(1-\varepsilon)^{n-p}}

Then
eq.

3.19
follows by arbitrariness of
\varepsilon>0
, keeping in mind the characterisation of
\AVR(g)
in (
2.17
) and the relation between the
p
-capacity and the normalised
p
-capacity.

Suppose now
(M,g)
is
\mathscr{C}^{0,\alpha}
-Asymptotically Conical for
\alpha>0
. By
theorem

2.1

u_{s}\in\mathscr{C}_{\mathrm{loc}}^{1,\beta}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)
for some
\beta\in(0,\alpha)
and for every
K\subset(1/s,+\infty)\times L
there exists constant
\mathrm{C}>0
such that

(3.25)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\|{{u_{s}}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}(K)$}}}}{\|{{u_{s}}}\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}(K)$}}}}{\left\|{{u_{s}}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}(K)$}}}}{\left\|{{u_{s}}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{1,\beta}(K)$}}}}\leq\mathrm{C}\mathchoice{\left\|{{u_{s}}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}}}{\|{{u_{s}}}\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}}}{\left\|{{u_{s}}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}}}{\left\|{{u_{s}}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}}}.

Since the metric
g_{(s)}
locally
\mathscr{C}^{0,\alpha}
-converges to
\hat{g}
on

by

2.14
, the constant in

3.25
can be chosen not depending on

. Hence, by Arzelà-Ascoli Theorem,
(u_{s})_{s\geq 1}

\mathscr{C}^{1}
-locally converges on
(0,+\infty)\times L
to the function

(3.26)

\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\rho^{-\frac{n-p}{p-1}}

as

. This proves
theorem

3.1
for
k=0
and
\ell\leq 1
.

If
(M,g)
is
\mathscr{C}^{k,\alpha}
-Asymptotically Conical for
k\geq 1
and
\alpha>0
, we already proved that (
3.1
) holds for
\ell\leq 1
. In particular, for every

there exists
S>0
such that
\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}u_{s}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}u_{s}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}u_{s}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}u_{s}$}}\right|$}}>0
holds on every compact

for every

. Applying

,
u_{s}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}((R,+\infty)\times L)
for every

. Moreover, for every
K\subset(R,+\infty)\times L)
there exists a constant
\mathrm{C}>0
such that

(3.27)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\|{{u_{s}}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{k+1,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{k+1,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k+1,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k+1,\alpha}(K)$}}}}{\|{{u_{s}}}\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{k+1,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{k+1,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k+1,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k+1,\alpha}(K)$}}}}{\left\|{{u_{s}}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{k+1,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{k+1,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k+1,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k+1,\alpha}(K)$}}}}{\left\|{{u_{s}}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{k+1,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{k+1,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k+1,\alpha}(K)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{k+1,\alpha}(K)$}}}}\leq\mathrm{C}\mathchoice{\left\|{{u_{s}}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}}}{\|{{u_{s}}}\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-1.50694pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}}}{\left\|{{u_{s}}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}}}{\left\|{{u_{s}}}\right\|_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\displaystyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\textstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}{\raisebox{-0.60275pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathscr{C}^{0}((1/s,+\infty)\times L)$}}}}.

Since
g_{(s)}
locally
\mathscr{C}^{k+1,\alpha}
-converges to
\hat{g}
on
(0,+\infty)\times L
, the constant in
eq.

3.27
can be chosen not depending on
s
. Since

is arbitrary,
(u_{s})_{s\geq 1}
is precompact with respect to the local
\mathscr{C}^{k+1}
-topology. Hence,
(u_{s})_{s\geq 1}
converges on compact subsets of
(0,+\infty)\times L
up to its
(k+1)
-th derivative to the function defined in
eq.

3.26
as
