, concluding the proof of
theorem

3.1
. ∎

As a consequence of
theorem

1.1
, we extend
lemma

2.16
showing that the volume of level sets of a suitable function of the
p
-capacitary potential behaves like the volume of geodesic balls approaching infinity. Requiring the assumption of
theorem

3.1
we actually deduce that the same occur for the
(n-1)
-Hausdorff measure of the level sets.

Proposition 3.4

(Asymptotic behaviour of the area of level sets)
.

In the same assumptions and notations of
theorem

1.1
, set, for
i=1,\dots,N
,

(3.28)

v_{i}=\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}^{(i)}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g;E_{i})}\right)^{\frac{1}{n-p}}u^{-\frac{p-1}{n-p}}.

Then, we have

(3.29)

\AVR(g;E_{i})=\lim_{s\to+\infty}\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v_{i}\leq s}\right\}}{\{{v_{i}\leq s}\}}{\left\{{v_{i}\leq s}\right\}}{\left\{{v_{i}\leq s}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v_{i}\leq s}\right\}}{\{{v_{i}\leq s}\}}{\left\{{v_{i}\leq s}\right\}}{\left\{{v_{i}\leq s}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v_{i}\leq s}\right\}}{\{{v_{i}\leq s}\}}{\left\{{v_{i}\leq s}\right\}}{\left\{{v_{i}\leq s}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v_{i}\leq s}\right\}}{\{{v_{i}\leq s}\}}{\left\{{v_{i}\leq s}\right\}}{\left\{{v_{i}\leq s}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}}{s^{n}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}}.

Moreover, if in addition
(M,g)
is
\mathscr{C}^{0,\alpha}
-Asymptotically Conical for
\alpha\in(0,1)
, then

(3.30)

\AVR(g;E_{i})=\lim_{s\to+\infty}\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\{v_{i}=s\}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\{v_{i}=s\}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\{v_{i}=s\}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\{v_{i}=s\}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}}{s^{n-1}\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}.

We prove the statement in the case
M
has only one end, being the general case a direct consequence. We drop the subscript
i
in the following lines. By
theorem

1.1
, for any
\varepsilon>0
there exists
R_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

(3.31)

(1-\varepsilon)\rho\leq v\leq(1+\varepsilon)\rho

holds on
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\geq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\geq R_{\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq R_{\varepsilon}}\right\}}
. Thus we have that

(3.32)

\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}\subset\mathchoice{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\{{v\leq s}\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}\subset\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}.

By the monotonicty of the volume we have that

(3.33)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}\leq\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\{{v\leq s}\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\{{v\leq s}\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\{{v\leq s}\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\{{v\leq s}\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}\leq\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}.

dividing each side by
s^{n}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}
and passing to the limit as
s\to+\infty
we can conclude that

(3.34)

\frac{\AVR(g)}{(1+\varepsilon)^{n}}\leq\lim_{s\to+\infty}\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\{{v\leq s}\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\{{v\leq s}\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\{{v\leq s}\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\{{v\leq s}\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}{\left\{{v\leq s}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}}{s^{n}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{B}^{n}$}}\right|$}}}\leq\frac{\AVR(g)}{(1-\varepsilon)^{n}}

by arbitrariness of
\varepsilon>0
we have that the volume of level sets behaves like the one of geodesic balls approaching infinity, proving the first identity in
eq.

3.29
. A straightforward computation relying on the identity

(3.35)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|$}}=\left(\frac{\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)}{\AVR(g)}\right)^{\frac{1}{n-p}}\left(\frac{p-1}{n-p}\right)u^{-\frac{n-1}{p-1}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}u$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}u$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}u$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}u$}}\right|$}}

permits to write

(3.36)

\AVR(g)=\frac{1}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}s^{n-1}}\int\limits_{\mathchoice{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}{\{{v=s}\}}{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\,\rm d}\sigma.

If in addition
(M,g)
is
\mathscr{C}^{0,\alpha}
-Asymptotically Conical for
\alpha\in(0,1)
, then
\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|
approaches
1
at infinity, hence we have

(3.37)

\AVR(g)=\lim_{s\to+\infty}\frac{1}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}s^{n-1}}\int\limits_{\mathchoice{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}{\{{v=s}\}}{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|^{p-1}$}}{\,\rm d}\sigma=\lim_{s\to+\infty}\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}{\{{v=s}\}}{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}{\{{v=s}\}}{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}{\{{v=s}\}}{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}{\{{v=s}\}}{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}{\left\{{v=s}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}}{s^{n-1}\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}

which concludes the proof of
eq.

3.30
.
∎

4.
Asymptotic behaviour of the weak IMCF

We give the precise definition of (proper) weak Inverse Mean Curvature Flow (IMCF for short).

Given an open set
U\subseteq M
,
we say that
w\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U)
is a weak IMCF if
for every
v\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U)
with
\{w\neq v\}\Subset U
and any compact set
K\subset U
containing
\{w\neq v\}
, we have

(4.1)

J_{w}^{K}(w)\leq J_{w}^{K}(v)

where

(4.2)

J_{w}^{K}(v)=\int_{K}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}v$}}\right|$}}+v\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}w$}}\right|$}}{\,\rm d}\mu.

Moreover, given a bounded open set
\Omega\subset M
with smooth boundary we say that
w
is a weak IMCF starting at
\Omega
if
for every
v\in\mathrm{Lip}_{\mathrm{loc}}(M)
with
\{w\neq v\}\Subset M\setminus{\Omega}
and any compact set
K\subset M\setminus\Omega
containing
\{w\neq v\}
we have

(4.3)

J_{w}^{K}(w)\leq J_{w}^{K}(v),

and the set
\Omega
is the
0
-sublevel set of
w
, that is

(4.4)

\Omega=\{w<0\}.

We say that
w
is a proper weak IMCF if the function
w
is proper, that is, its sublevel sets are precompact.
In the following result we gather the existence and the fundamental estimates for
w
in the Asymptotically Conical setting.

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
(M,g)
be a
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifold satisfying the Ricci curvature bound (
1.3
). Let
\Omega\subset M
be an open bounded subset with smooth boundary. Then, there exists a proper weak IMCF
w
starting at
\Omega
. Moreover, given
o\in\Omega
, the function
w
satisfies

(4.5)

\displaystyle(n-1)\log\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(x,o)-\mathrm{C}\leq w\leq(n-1)\log\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(x,o)+\mathrm{C}

where
\mathrm{C}=\mathrm{C}(M,n,\Omega)
.

The existence is guaranteed by
[
MRS21
, Theorem 1.7]
whose assumptions are satisfied in virtue of
eq.

2.41
and
eq.

1.3
. The lower bound is the consequence of
[
MRS21
, Theorem 1.7 and 1.3]
. Let
R
be such that

(4.6)

-(p-1)\log G_{p}(x,o)\leq(n-p)\log\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(x,o)-\log\mathrm{C}_{L}

on
M\smallsetminus B(o,R)
with the constant
\mathrm{C}_{L}=\mathrm{C}\,a\,{\rm Cap}_{p}(\overline{K};E)
described in the statement of
theorem

2.24
. By
[
MRS21
]
,
-(p-1)\log G_{p}(x,o)
locally uniformly converges as
p\to 1^{+}
. Then we can choose
a
in the constant
\mathrm{C}_{L}
independent from
p
so that
G_{p}(o,x)\geq a^{\frac{1}{p-1}}
holds on
M\smallsetminus K
where
K
is as in
definition

2.13
. Moreover, by
eq.

1.13
, since
\partial K
is smooth,
{\rm Cap}_{p}(\overline{K};E)
is bounded as
p\to 1^{+}
. Hence the constant
\mathrm{C}_{L}
in
eq.

4.6
does not depend on
p
. Passing to the limit as
p\to 1^{+}
, in virtue of the upper bound in
[
MRS21
, Theorem 1.7]
, we obtain

(4.7)

w\leq(n-1)\log\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(x,o)+\mathrm{C}

outside some
B(o,R)
. Since both the left and side and the right hand side are continuous, the bound can be extended to
M\smallsetminus\Omega
. ∎

In
[
MRS21
, Remark 4.9]
the authors also obtained a gradient bound that reads as

(4.8)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}w$}}\right|$}}\leq\frac{\mathrm{C}}{\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(x,o)^{1/\kappa^{\prime}}},

\displaystyle\text{where }\kappa^{\prime}=\frac{1+\sqrt{1+4\kappa^{2}}}{2}\geq 1,

for some constant
\mathrm{C}>0
depending only on
\Omega
, the dimension
n
and the geometry of the ambient manifold. By
[
GW79
]
(see also
[
MRS21
, Remark 4.5]
) The exponent
\kappa^{\prime}
can be chosen equal to
1
if the lower bound on the Ricci curvature is of the kind
\Ric\geq-(n-1)f(\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(x,o))
for some smooth nonnegative function
f(t)
, such that

(4.9)

\int\limits_{0}^{+\infty}tf(t){\,\rm d}t<+\infty.

The Weak Existence Theorem 3.1
[
HI01
]
the function
w
satisfies

(4.10)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}w$}}\right|$}}(x)\leq\sup_{\partial\Omega\cap B(x,r)}{\rm H}+\frac{\mathrm{C}}{r}

for almost every
x\in M\smallsetminus\Omega
and for every
r
for which there exists a function
\psi\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(B(x,r))
such that
\psi\geq\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(x,\,\cdot\,)^{2}
,
\psi(x)=0
,
\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\psi$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\psi$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\psi$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\psi$}}\right|$}}\leq 3\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(x,\,\cdot\,)
,
{\rm D}^{2}\psi\leq 3g
and
\Ric\geq-\mathrm{C}/r^{2}
in
B(x,r)
. The existence of
\psi
is guaranteed if a sectional curvature lower bound is ensured. Otherwise, one can require a higher rate of convergence of the metric.

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
(M,g)
be a
\mathscr{C}^{1}
-Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifold satisfying the Ricci curvature bound (
1.3
). Let
\Omega\subset M
be an open bounded subset with smooth boundary,
o\in M
. There exists a postive constant
\mathrm{C}=\mathrm{C}(n,M,\Omega)>0
, such that the solution
w\in\mathrm{Lip}(M)
of the weak IMCF starting at
\Omega
, given by
theorem

4.1
satisfies

(4.11)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}w$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}w$}}\right|$}}(x)\leq\frac{\mathrm{C}}{\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(x,o)}

for almost every
x\in M\smallsetminus\Omega
.

By
[
HI01
, Weak Existence Theorem 3.1]
the function
w
satisfies (
4.10
) for almost every
x\in M\smallsetminus\Omega
. In virtue of the discussion in
[
HI01
, Definition 3.3]
(see also the proof of
[
HI01
, Blowdown Lemma 7.1]
) there exists a constant
\mathrm{C}>0
and
R>0
such that
r\geq\mathrm{C}\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits(x,o)
in (
4.10
) for every
x\in M\smallsetminus B(o,R)
. Then (
4.11
) follows taking
r
so that
\partial\Omega\cap B(x,r)=\varnothing
. ∎The notion of weak IMCF is intimately tied with that of strictly outward minimising sets
[
HI01
, Minimizing Hulls, p.371]
. Leaving the details to
[
FM20
]
, we recall that a bounded set
E\subset M
is strictly outward minimising if
P(F)>P(E)
for any bounded
F\supset E
with
\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle F\setminus E$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle F\setminus E$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle F\setminus E$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F\setminus E$}}\right|$}}>0
. The following simple lemma implies the existence of a foliation of strictly outward minimising sets in Asymptotically Conical manifolds.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
(M,g)
be a
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifold. Then
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq r}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}
is strictly outward minimising for
r
large enough.

Consider any
\varphi\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\geq r}\right\}}{\{{\rho\geq r}\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq r}\right\}})
, then

(4.12)

\displaystyle\int\limits_{\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\geq r}\right\}}{\{{\rho\geq r}\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq r}\right\}}}\div\left(\frac{{\rm D}\rho}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|$}}}\right)\varphi{\,\rm d}{\mu}=-\int\limits_{\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\geq r}\right\}}{\{{\rho\geq r}\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\geq r}\right\}}}\mathchoice{\left\langle\vphantom{{{\rm D}\varphi}}{\frac{{\rm D}\rho}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|$}}}}\kern 2.0pt\right|\left.\kern-1.0pt{{\rm D}\varphi}\vphantom{{\frac{{\rm D}\rho}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.75pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.75pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.25pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|$}}}}}\right\rangle}{\langle{\frac{{\rm D}\rho}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle{\rm D}\rho$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle{\rm \mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}{\{{\rho=r}\}}{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}{\{{\rho=r}\}}{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}{\{{\rho=r}\}}{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}{\{{\rho=r}\}}{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=r}\right\}}$}}\right|_{g}$}}\leq\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|_{g}$}}
and the equality holds true if and only if
\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle F\smallsetminus\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho<r}\right\}}{\{{\rho<r}\}}{\left\{{\rho<r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho<r}\right\}}$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle F\smallsetminus\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho<r}\right\}}{\{{\rho<r}\}}{\left\{{\rho<r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho<r}\right\}}$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle F\smallsetminus\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho<r}\right\}}{\{{\rho<r}\}}{\left\{{\rho<r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho<r}\right\}}$}}\right|_{g}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle F\smallsetminus\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho<r}\right\}}{\{{\rho<r}\}}{\left\{{\rho<r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho<r}\right\}}$}}\right|_{g}$}}=0
, which gives that
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq r}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r}\right\}}
is strictly outward minimising. ∎

By
[
FM20
, Theorem 2.16]
the existence of an exhaustion of strictly outward minimising sets provides, for any bounded
\Omega\subset M
with smooth boundary, a suitable bounded
strictly outward minimising hull

\Omega^{*}
, that in particular fulfils

(4.19)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}=\inf\mathchoice{\left\{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}\left.\vphantom{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}F\text{ is bounded and }\Omega\subseteq F}\right|F\text{ is bounded and }\Omega\subseteq F}\right\}}{\{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}\left.\vphantom{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}F\text{ is bounded and }\Omega\subseteq F}\right|F\text{ is bounded and }\Omega\subseteq F}\}}{\left\{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}\left.\vphantom{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}F\text{ is bounded and }\Omega\subseteq F}\right|F\text{ is bounded and }\Omega\subseteq F}\right\}}{\left\{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}\left.\vphantom{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial^{*}F$}}\right|$}}F\text{ is bounded and }\Omega\subseteq F}\right|F\text{ is bounded and }\Omega\subseteq F}\right\}}.

Let
(M,g)
be a
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifold defined in
definition

2.13
and denote by
E_{1},\ldots,E_{N}
the (finitely many) ends. Consider
\Omega\subset M
open bounded subset with smooth boundary and
w:M\smallsetminus\Omega\to[0,+\infty)
the weak IMCF
w
starting at
\Omega
. As we did for the
p
-capacity we can define the area of the strictly outward minimising hull of
\Omega
with respect to one end
E_{i}
. Indeed, there exists a time
T
such that
\mathchoice{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\{{w\leq t}\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}
contains the compact
K
defined in
definition

2.13
for every
t\geq T
. We then define the area of
\partial\Omega^{*}
with respect to
E_{i}
as

(4.20)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}=\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\mathchoice{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\{{w\leq t}\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\mathchoice{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\{{w\leq t}\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\mathchoice{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\{{w\leq t}\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\mathchoice{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\{{w\leq t}\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}}{\mathrm{e}^{t}}

for some
t\geq T
. Observe that such definition is well posed by
[
HI01
, Exponential Growth Lemma 1.6]
. Moreover, it can be checked, substantially using
[
FM20
, Proposition 3.4]
, that
\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|
splits as

(4.21)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}.

Theorem 1.2 in
[
FM20
]
establishes a relation between
\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)
and
\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|
. The same relation between
\mathrm{C}^{(i)}_{p}(\Omega)
and
\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}
holds true.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
(M,g)
be a
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-Asymptotically Conical Riemannian manifold with Ricci curvature satisfying (
1.3
) and
N
be the number of ends. Let
\Omega\subseteq M
be an open bounded subset with smooth boundary. Then,

(4.22)

\lim_{p\to 1^{+}}\mathrm{C}^{(i)}_{p}(\Omega)=\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}

holds for every
i=1,\ldots,N

Let
w
be the solution to the weak IMCF starting at
\Omega
and
T
large enough so that
\mathchoice{\left\{{w\leq T}\right\}}{\{{w\leq T}\}}{\left\{{w\leq T}\right\}}{\left\{{w\leq T}\right\}}
contains
K
in
definition

2.13
. Given
u_{p}
the solution to (
1.2
), by
[
MRS21
]
we have
-(p-1)\log u_{p}
where converges locally uniformly to
w
as
p\to 1^{+}
. In particular, for every
t\geq T
there exists
p_{t}\in(1,n)
such that
\mathchoice{\left\{{w\leq T}\right\}}{\{{w\leq T}\}}{\left\{{w\leq T}\right\}}{\left\{{w\leq T}\right\}}\subseteq\mathchoice{\left\{{-(1-p)\log u_{p}\leq t}\right\}}{\{{-(1-p)\log u_{p}\leq t}\}}{\left\{{-(1-p)\log u_{p}\leq t}\right\}}{\left\{{-(1-p)\log u_{p}\leq t}\right\}}
holds for every
p<p_{t}
. Arguing as in
[
FM20
, Theorem 1.2]
, since an Isoperimetric Inequality is in force by
proposition

2.20
, we can prove that
\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\mathchoice{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\{{w\leq t}\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\mathchoice{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\{{w\leq t}\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\mathchoice{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\{{w\leq t}\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\mathchoice{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\{{w\leq t}\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}\leq\mathrm{C}_{n,p}\,{\rm Cap}_{p}(\partial\mathchoice{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\{{w\leq t}\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}}{\left\{{w\leq t}\right\}};E_{i})
, for some constant
\mathrm{C}_{n,p}
such that
\mathrm{C}_{n,p}\to 1
as
p\to 1^{+}
. In particular, by the monotonicity of the
p
-capacity and
proposition

2.12
we have that

(4.23)

\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{C}_{n,p}\,\mathrm{e}^{-T}{\rm Cap}_{p}\left(\mathchoice{\left\{{w\leq T}\right\}}{\{{w\leq T}\}}{\left\{{w\leq T}\right\}}{\left\{{w\leq T}\right\}}\cap E_{i};E_{i}\right)\leq\mathrm{e}^{-T}{\rm Cap}_{p}\left(\mathchoice{\left\{{u_{p}\geq\mathrm{C}_{n,p}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{t}{p-1}}}\right\}}{\{{u_{p}\geq\mathrm{C}_{n,p}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{t}{p-1}}}\}}{\left\{{u_{p}\geq\mathrm{C}_{n,p}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{t}{p-1}}}\right\}}{\left\{{u_{p}\geq\mathrm{C}_{n,p}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{t}{p-1}}}\right\}}\cap E_{i};E_{i}\right)

(4.24)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{C}_{n,p}\,\mathrm{e}^{t-2T}{\rm Cap}_{p}\left(\mathchoice{\left\{{u_{p}\geq\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{T}{p-1}}}\right\}}{\{{u_{p}\geq\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{T}{p-1}}}\}}{\left\{{u_{p}\geq\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{T}{p-1}}}\right\}}{\left\{{u_{p}\geq\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{T}{p-1}}}\right\}}\cap E_{i};E_{i}\right)\leq\mathrm{C}_{n,p}\mathrm{e}^{t-T}{\rm Cap}_{p}^{(i)}(\Omega)

Sending
p\to 1^{+}
and then
t\to T^{+}
we have that

(4.25)

\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}\leq\lim_{p\to 1^{+}}\mathrm{C}_{p}^{(i)}(\Omega).

If for some
i=1,\ldots,N
the inequality is strict, then

(4.26)

\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}<\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lim_{p\to 1^{+}}\mathrm{C}_{p}^{(i)}(\Omega)=\lim_{p\to 1^{+}}\mathrm{C}_{p}(\Omega)=\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}

which is a contradiction. ∎

Clearly, we also obtain the analogous of
proposition

3.2
.

Proposition 4.5

(Asymptotic behaviour of the area of level sets)
.

In the same assumptions and notations of
theorem

1.2
, set, for
i=1,\ldots,m
,

(4.27)

v_{i}=\left(\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|^{(i)}$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}\AVR(g;E_{i})}\right)^{\frac{1}{n-1}}\mathrm{e}^{\frac{w}{n-1}}.

Then, we have

(4.28)

\lim_{s\to+\infty}\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v_{i}=s}\right\}}{\{{v_{i}=s}\}}{\left\{{v_{i}=s}\right\}}{\left\{{v_{i}=s}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v_{i}=s}\right\}}{\{{v_{i}=s}\}}{\left\{{v_{i}=s}\right\}}{\left\{{v_{i}=s}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v_{i}=s}\right\}}{\{{v_{i}=s}\}}{\left\{{v_{i}=s}\right\}}{\left\{{v_{i}=s}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{v_{i}=s}\right\}}{\{{v_{i}=s}\}}{\left\{{v_{i}=s}\right\}}{\left\{{v_{i}=s}\right\}}\cap E_{i}$}}\right|$}}}{s^{n-1}\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}=\AVR(g).

Observe that a similar result for the
p
-capacitary potential was obtained in
eq.

3.30
. In that case a first order asymptotic behaviour for the
p
-capacitary potential was required in the proof. The reason is that the area is linked to the level sets of IMCF in the same way the
p
-capacity is linked to the level set of
p
-capacitary potential. A simple
\mathscr{C}^{0}
-convergence is therefore enough in this case.

As a byproduct we also obtain the counterpart of
proposition

3.3
proving that

(4.29)

\displaystyle w(x)=(n-1)\log(\rho(x))

\displaystyle\text{with }x\in(0,+\infty)\times L

is the unique solution on
(0,+\infty)\times L
up to a constant.

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
((0,+\infty)\times L,\hat{g})
be a Riemannian cone with nonnegative Ricci curvature where
L
is a closed connected smooth hypersurface. Let
w
be a weak IMCF on
(0,+\infty)\times L
satisfying
w(x)\geq(n-1)\log\rho(x)+\mathrm{C}
for every
x\in(0,+\infty)\times L
for some constant
\mathrm{C}\geq 0
. Then, there exists a
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
such that

(4.30)

\displaystyle w(x)=(n-1)\log(\rho(x))+\gamma

\displaystyle\text{with }x\in(0,+\infty)\times L,

holds on
(0,+\infty)\times L
.

In the case of the flat Euclidean space, this uniqueness result has been obtained in
[
HI01
, Proposition 7.2]
.

The proof follows the same lines of
theorem

1.1
. We prove the theorem in the case
M
has only one end, since the general case follows applying the result to each end. We denote by
g_{(s)}
the metric
s^{-2}\omega_{s}^{*}g
on
[1/s,+\infty)\times L
, being
\omega_{s}
the family of diffeomorphism defined in
eq.

2.20
. We divide the proof in three steps. The second and the third one contains the proof of
propositions

4.5
and
4.6
respectively.

Define for every
s\geq 1
the family of functions
w_{s}:[1/s,+\infty)\times L\to\mathbb{R}
as

(4.31)

w_{s}=w\circ\omega_{s}-(n-1)\log(s)

where
\omega_{s}
is the map defined in
eq.

2.20
. Employing (
4.5
) in
theorem

4.1
and
proposition

4.2
as in the proof of
theorem

1.1
, it is easy to show that
(w_{s})_{s\geq 1}
is equibounded and equi-Lipschitz. By Arzelà-Ascoli Theorem,
(w_{s})_{s\geq 1}
is precompact with respect to the local uniform convergence on
(0,+\infty)\times L
. Moreover, by
[
HI01
, Compactness Theorem 2.1]
every limit point
u
is a solution to the (weak) IMCF on
(0,+\infty)\times L
and by
eq.

4.5
there exists a positive constant
\mathrm{C}>0
such that

(4.32)

(n-1)\log(\rho(x))-\mathrm{C}\leq u(x)\leq(n-1)\log(\rho(x))+\mathrm{C}

is satisfied on
(0,+\infty)\times L
.

Here we prove
proposition

4.6
, inferring in particular that any limit point
v
of
(w_{s})_{s\geq 1}
satisfies

(4.33)

v(x)=(n-1)\log\rho(x)+\gamma

on
(0,+\infty)\times L
for some
\gamma\in\mathbb{R}
. Let
\mathfrak{e}_{v}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be defined as

(4.34)

\mathfrak{e}_{v}(t)=\frac{R(t)}{r(t)}

where, for every
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
[r(t),R(t)]\times L
is the smallest annulus containing
\mathchoice{\left\{{v=t}\right\}}{\{{v=t}\}}{\left\{{v=t}\right\}}{\left\{{v=t}\right\}}
. Arguing as in
section

3
of
theorem

1.1
starting from any weak IMCF
v
on
(0,+\infty)\times L
we can produce a function
u:(0,+\infty)\times L\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\mathfrak{e}_{u}(t)
is constant and is equal to
\mathfrak{e}_{u}=\sup_{t}\mathfrak{e}_{v}(t)\in[1,+\infty)
. Suppose by contradiction that
\mathfrak{e}_{u}>1
. Then the level
\mathchoice{\left\{{u=0}\right\}}{\{{u=0}\}}{\left\{{u=0}\right\}}{\left\{{u=0}\right\}}\subset[r(0),\mathfrak{e}_{u}r(0)]\times L
and touches both the cross-sections
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=r(0)}\right\}}{\{{\rho=r(0)}\}}{\left\{{\rho=r(0)}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=r(0)}\right\}}
and
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=\mathfrak{e}_{u}r(0)}\right\}}{\{{\rho=\mathfrak{e}_{u}r(0)}\}}{\left\{{\rho=\mathfrak{e}_{u}r(0)}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=\mathfrak{e}_{u}r(0)}\right\}}
without being equal to either one. We aim to compare the weak flow and the two strong flows and prove that the level sets of
u
detach from the spheres. Perturb
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq r(0)}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq r(0)}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r(0)}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r(0)}\right\}}
outward and
\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\mathfrak{e}_{u}r(0)}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\mathfrak{e}_{u}r(0)}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\mathfrak{e}_{u}r(0)}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\mathfrak{e}_{u}r(0)}\right\}}
inward to obtain
D^{-}
and
D^{+}
respectively with the following properties:

•

\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq r(0)}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq r(0)}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r(0)}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq r(0)}\right\}}\subset D^{-}\subset\mathchoice{\left\{{u\leq 0}\right\}}{\{{u\leq 0}\}}{\left\{{u\leq 0}\right\}}{\left\{{u\leq 0}\right\}}
and
\mathchoice{\left\{{u\leq 0}\right\}}{\{{u\leq 0}\}}{\left\{{u\leq 0}\right\}}{\left\{{u\leq 0}\right\}}\subset D^{+}\subset\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\mathfrak{e}_{u}r(0)}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\mathfrak{e}_{u}r(0)}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\mathfrak{e}_{u}r(0)}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\mathfrak{e}_{u}r(0)}\right\}}
;

•

D^{-}
and
D^{+}
are starshaped with smooth strictly mean convex boundary.

Then the smooth IMCF starting at
D^{+}
and
D^{-}
exists for all time by
[
Zho18
, Theorem 3.1]
and by
[
HI01
, Smooth Flow Lemma 2.3]
it coincides with the weak notion of the IMCF. Denote by
(D^{-}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
and
(D^{+}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
the sublevel sets of the two weak (and smooth) IMCF starting at
D^{-}
and
D^{+}
respectively. By the Strong Comparison Principle for smooth flows we have that

(4.35)

\displaystyle\rho(x)>r(0)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{t}{n-1}}\text{ for }x\in\partial D^{-}_{t}

and

\displaystyle\rho(x)<\mathfrak{e}_{u}r(0)\mathrm{e}^{\frac{t}{n-1}}\text{ for }x\in\partial D^{+}_{t}.

On the other hand, by the Weak Comparison theorem
[
HI01
, Theorem 2.2(ii)]

(4.36)

D^{-}_{t}\subset\mathchoice{\left\{{u\leq t}\right\}}{\{{u\leq t}\}}{\left\{{u\leq t}\right\}}{\left\{{u\leq t}\right\}}\subset D^{+}_{t}.

Coupling (
4.35
) and (
4.36
) we have that
\mathfrak{e}_{u}(t)<\mathfrak{e}_{u}
which is the desired contradiction. Then
\mathfrak{e}_{u}=1
that completes, as in
section

3
of
theorem

1.1
, the proof of
proposition

4.6
.

Let
v=(n-1)\log\rho+\gamma
be a limit point of the family
(w_{s})_{s\geq 1}
. We are now going to prove that

(4.37)

\gamma=\log\left(\frac{\AVR(g)\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}}\right).

The characterisation proves
proposition

4.5
and implies that the limit point is unique, concluding the proof. We work with the auxiliary function

(4.38)

u=\mathrm{e}^{\frac{w-\gamma}{n-1}}

Since
v
is a limit point for the family
(w_{s})_{s\geq 1}
there exists a subsequence
(w_{s_{k}})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
,
s_{k}
increasing and divergent as
k\to+\infty
, such that
w_{s_{k}}\to v=(n-1)\log\rho+\gamma
locally uniformly on
(0,+\infty)\times L
as
k\to+\infty
. Then for any
\varepsilon>0
there exists
k_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(4.39)

\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}\subset\mathchoice{\left\{{u\leq s_{k}}\right\}}{\{{u\leq s_{k}}\}}{\left\{{u\leq s_{k}}\right\}}{\left\{{u\leq s_{k}}\right\}}\subset\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho\leq\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}

holds for every
k\geq k_{\varepsilon}
. By
lemma

4.3
we can assume
k_{\varepsilon}
large enough so that both the left most and the right most sets are strictly outward minimising for any
k\geq k_{\varepsilon}
. Then the perimeter is monotone by inclusion and by
[
HI01
, Exponential Growth Lemma 1.6]
we have

(4.40)

\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1+\varepsilon}}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}\leq\mathrm{e}^{\gamma}s_{k}^{n-1}\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}\leq\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\}}{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}{\left\{{\rho=\frac{s_{k}}{1-\varepsilon}}\right\}}$}}\right|$}}

Dividing both sides by
\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}s_{k}^{n-1}
, sending
k\to+\infty
and using
lemma

2.16
we infer that

(4.41)

\frac{\AVR(g)}{(1+\varepsilon)^{n-1}}\leq\mathrm{e}^{\gamma}\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}\leq\frac{\AVR(g)}{(1-\varepsilon)^{n-1}}

Then (
4.37
) follows by arbitrariness of
\varepsilon>0
. ∎

Firstly, observe that we do not have the analogous of
theorem

3.1
for the IMCF. The asymptotic behaviour of higher order derivatives of the
p
-capacitary potential is indeed a consequence of the higher regularity one gets once shown that the gradient does not vanish anymore sufficiently far out. In the case of the weak IMCF, it is not clear to us how to work out this last step.

The result above is to be compared with
[
HI01
, Lemma 7.1]
. We obtain here an explicit characterisation of the constants
c_{\lambda}
that is

(4.42)

c_{\lambda}=-(n-1)\log\left(\frac{\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}}\lambda\right).

The constant appearing in
eq.

1.12
satisfies

(4.43)

\log\left(\frac{\AVR(g)\mathchoice{\raisebox{3.07002pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-3.07002pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.82002pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.82002pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.24501pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.24501pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.70833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.70833pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathbb{S}^{n-1}$}}\right|$}}}{\mathchoice{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\displaystyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\displaystyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{2.5pt}{$\textstyle\left|\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\hbox{$\textstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.79709pt}{$\scriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.79709pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}{\raisebox{1.2604pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\left|\raisebox{-1.2604pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\partial\Omega^{*}$}}\right|$}}}\right)=\lim_{p\to 1^{+}}-(p-1)\log\left[\left(\frac{{\rm Cap}_{p}(\partial\Omega)}{\AVR(g)}\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\right]

thanks to
eq.

1.13
, which is the constant in
eq.

1.8
, trasformed accordingly to
w_{p}=-(p-1)\log u_{p}
. Hence, even if by
theorem

4.1

w_{p}\to w
only locally uniformly as
p\to 1^{+}
, the asymptotic behaviour of
w
is anyway affected by this procedure.
